Cartograph · architecture and implementation plan, 22 Nov 2026

One binary, eight phases, every item in order

This page replaces the earlier ontology review. It merges five analysts' breakdowns (indexer, server, cross-service links, AI, front end and hardening) into one plan that follows the CLI and MCP spec and the decision record (A1 to I9). Every reference between areas is resolved to an item id, every duplicate has one owner, and every conflict the analysts raised has a ruling. Each item keeps its full text behind a fold.

151items, 34 S · 79 M · 38 L
8phases, 0 to 7
30conflicts decided
8need your call

Sizes: S is 1 to 2 days, M about a week, L two weeks or more. Summed that way the plan is about 184 item-weeks of work. That is a measure of volume, not a schedule: phases 5 and 6 run side by side, and most phases have work for three or four people at once.

The system at a glance

components and trust boundaries · A1–A4 · B1 · F4 · G3
Trust boundaryOutside the machineThe one write path, and the one outbound callReads
A1

One query layer, four readers

HTTP handlers, MCP tools, CLI commands and Ask tools are thin wrappers over cartograph-query. None holds SQL. The layer opens the index read-only, so a bug in a reader can't change it (SRV-02).

B2

One writer

Only the indexer writes index.cartograph, under an OS lock the kernel releases if the process dies. A second request sets a dirty flag and exits 0 (IDX-13, IDX-15).

A3

The browser is outside the binary's trust

Every API request passes the Host allow list, the Origin check and a bearer token that reached the page in the URL fragment. There are no cookies and no CORS (SRV-05, HD-11).

F8

Repo text is data

Repositories, the committed manifest, the override file, adapters, SCIP files and share links are all untrusted input. Their text never reaches tool descriptions, server instructions, CARTOGRAPH.md or the system prompt.

G3

One way out

The only connection Cartograph opens to another machine is the model call from the Ask loop, to a base URL saved in settings and checked by the outbound policy (AI-09). git fetch runs through git and only with sync --fetch. There is no telemetry, no update check and no hosted service.

Data flow

index time · read time · question time
Index time discover→walk first-parent→extract once per blob id→resolve and replay→intervals in SQLite→derived rows by inputs_key

Each commit costs only what it changed. The reverse index re-resolves only references waiting on a path or config file that changed: 60,039 resolutions on remotion in place of 37.8 million (measured). Facts are cached by blob id, extractor and version, so a parser upgrade re-extracts only its own file types. Derived rows (layout, search tables, counts, coverage, summaries) rebuild only when their inputs_key changes. Owners: IDX-04 to IDX-07, IDX-21.

Read time freshness check, under 100 ms→query layer snapshot→answer with commits behind→background sync if behind

Every read answers from the current index straight away and says how far behind each repo is (D1). Readers hold a WAL snapshot for one request, never longer, so a sync is never blocked and never blocks. A slice at one level of detail carries its links' intervals, so the browser scrubs time with no request per frame.

Question time graph engine answers first→model may call five read tools→citation checker, four rules→repair once, or fall back to the engine

With no provider set, the engine's answer is the answer and nothing leaves the machine. With one, the model words it; every sentence must cite handles from this turn that still exist and cover the date asked about (G5).

From a client's call site to the code that handles it

This chain from the earlier page still holds, and is now owned. Each step adds evidence; the weakest step sets the link's level, and a step that fails becomes an unresolved record, not a guess.

StepExampleFound byItem
Call sitepricing.get(`/v2/prices/${sku}`)Client adapter, a tree-sitter queryXS-03
TemplateGET /v2/prices/{}Path-template normaliserXS-04
Base URLPRICING_URLEnv read and client base factsXS-09
Hosthttp://pricing.catalog.svcHelm values, compose, k8s files in indexed repos, per environmentXS-09
Servicecatalog/pricingHost alias to serviceXS-02, IDX-20
OperationgetPriceOpenAPI operation matched by templateXS-08
Handlerprices.py:18Framework adapterXS-15
LevelLikely · 0.62 and upEvidence class, then noisy-or inside the bandXS-05

Phase timeline

width follows the sum of item sizes
0Spikes and setup9 items · done 22 Nov 20261One repo in the browser18 items · about 19 item-weeks2A real workspace, kept current20 items · about 21 item-weeks3The whole app, and MCP28 items · about 40 item-weeks4Cross-service links27 items · about 38 item-weeks5Symbols, branches, impact8 items · about 16 item-weeks6Ask22 items · about 23 item-weeks7Harden and ship19 items · about 23 item-weeks

The order starts from the front-end and hardening plan's proposal and changes it in three ways. Its phase 2 is split in two, so a multi-repo workspace that stays current ships before the full app. Search tables (AI-15) move from the AI stage to phase 3, because the palette and MCP find need them. Service detection moves from the cross-service stage to phase 3, because level of detail needs services.

Reading the items: each row shows the id, the title, its size, what it depends on (resolved to real ids), a one- or two-line summary, and short security, scale and efficiency notes. "Full text" holds the analyst's complete entry. Route paths inside the full text are as the analyst wrote them; where they differ, the Interfaces section and the merge notes win.

Phase 0 · Spikes, benchmarks and setup

9 items · 6S 3M 0L · before roadmap stage 1
Goal

Measure every number a decision rests on, agree the HTTP contract, and put one lockfile, CI and the licence deny list in place before any product code.

What a user can do at the end

Nothing to use yet. A reader can open docs/research/ and see, for each of B7, H1, D1, I1, G3 and G4, a result marked holds or changed, with the fallback chosen where it changed.

Exit gate
  • oxc_resolver driven over a virtual file system reports file_dependencies and missing_dependencies, or the candidate-list fallback is chosen (IDX-02).
  • gix first-parent walk and rename detection agree with git log -M50% on remotion's 9,498 commits; time recorded against git log --raw and libgit2.
  • Freshness check for 30 repos under 100 ms, with no network syscall.
  • sigma 4 at 60k nodes and 200k links: p95 frame under 33 ms at workspace zoom, pick under 50 ms, load under 2 s, heap under 600 MB, on an integrated GPU (proposed marks).
  • Code2DFD fetched and its languages confirmed; the hand-labelled set is started (INT-02).
  • genai, keyring and ACP checked feature by feature, each fail with a named fallback (AI-01).
  • npm install fails naming pnpm; adding a GPL or AGPL crate fails CI; a failing test blocks merge on Linux, macOS and Windows.
Risks retired
  • The replay design depending on a resolver that can't be driven per commit (IDX R1).
  • Choosing gix, sigma or genai on faith.
  • Licence contamination from an AGPL layout crate (I2).
  • Five areas building against five different route tables.

One lockfile, pinned toolchains S

Depends on nothing

pnpm is the package manager: CI runs pnpm install --frozen-lockfile, pnpm-lock.yaml is the only tracked lockfile, and .gitignore already lists package-lock.json with the comment "this project uses pnpm". Delete the stray…

Done when: npm install fails with a message naming pnpm; CI fails if package-lock.json or yarn.lock is tracked; cargo build --locked is the only build command in CI.

Merge note: Owns the lockfile and toolchain pins. Merges the rust-toolchain.toml and Cargo.lock lines of IDX-01 and the pinned-actions line of SRV-29.

Security
one lockfile means one audited dependency set. SHA-pinned actions close the tag-retargeting attack.

Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-01

Depends on (as written)

nothing. Do it first.

What to build

pnpm is the package manager: CI runs pnpm install --frozen-lockfile, pnpm-lock.yaml is the only tracked lockfile, and .gitignore already lists package-lock.json with the comment "this project uses pnpm". Delete the stray untracked package-lock.json (3,630 lines) from working copies, add "packageManager": "pnpm@11.x.y+sha512…" to package.json, and add a preinstall guard (only-allow pnpm). For Rust: commit Cargo.lock, build with --locked, add rust-toolchain.toml. Pin every GitHub Action to a commit SHA (actions/checkout@v4 and the rest are tags today).

Done when

npm install fails with a message naming pnpm; CI fails if package-lock.json or yarn.lock is tracked; cargo build --locked is the only build command in CI.

Security

one lockfile means one audited dependency set. SHA-pinned actions close the tag-retargeting attack.

Full text of IDX-01

Depends on (as written)

nothing.

What to build

  • A cargo workspace at crates/ with these crates:
CrateHolds
cartograph-coreIds (RepoId, Seq, Oid, NodeId, LinkId), Error, config types, paths of the data folder
cartograph-discoveryWorkspace file, git checks, manifest, registry, default branch, services
cartograph-gitgix wrapper: walk, diff, blob reads, renames, merge base, HEAD reads
cartograph-parsetree-sitter grammars, extractors, the Fact types
cartograph-factsFact cache, resolvers, the replay engine
cartograph-storeSQLite schema, migrations, writer, lock, fact hash
cartograph-indexOrchestration: sync, rebuild, overlays, layout, freshness. Proposed addition to the brief's list, so that store and facts don't depend on each other.
cartograph-queryRead-only query layer (owned by the query area; this area supplies the schema)
cartograph-cliclap commands, output, exit codes (owned by the CLI area; this area supplies index, sync, status, verify, hooks, schedule back ends)
  • core::Error: thiserror enums per crate, each variant carrying what, why and action (E1), plus exit_code() mapping to 0, 1, 2, 3 (E2). anyhow only in cartograph-cli.
  • Logging with tracing. A rotating file in <data>/logs/ through tracing-appender (7 files, 10 MB each, proposed). Stderr only with -v. Structured fields repo, seq, oid, path, so cartograph report can drop path and code fields (X13).
  • CI: cargo fmt, clippy -D warnings, cargo test, cargo deny (licences and advisories), on macOS, Linux and Windows.
  • rust-toolchain.toml and a committed Cargo.lock.

Done when

cargo build --workspace and CI pass on three OSes; cargo deny check passes with GPL and AGPL denied; an error from any crate prints three lines (what, why, try) and the right exit code.

Security

supply chain. cargo deny with a licence allow-list (denies git2/libgit2 per B7 and the AGPL forceatlas2 crate per I2), cargo audit in CI, bundled SQLite (rusqlite feature bundled) so the system library can't be swapped. Logs never hold file contents. No network crates in the dependency tree of the indexing crates; a CI check greps cargo tree for reqwest, hyper client and ureq outside the Ask crate.

Scale

n/a.

Efficiency

lto = "thin", codegen-units = 1, panic = "abort" in release (proposed; abort needs IDX-13's crash safety). mimalloc as the allocator, pending IDX-02 numbers.

Size

S.

Tests

unit tests for the exit-code mapping and error rendering; a snapshot test (insta) of one rendered error.

HD-12

Supply chain: audit, licences, SBOM M

Depends on HD-01

cargo deny with a licence allow list. Deny AGPL and GPL, which enforces I2's warning about the forceatlas2 crate and I1's note on Gephi Lite; deny PolyForm, so no GitNexus code arrives through a dependency.

Done when: adding a GPL crate or a package with a known advisory fails CI; each release carries an SBOM that lists every crate and npm package in the binary.

Merge note: Owns licence, advisory and SBOM checks. Merges the cargo deny and cargo audit lines of IDX-01, SRV-29 and AI-07.

Security
threats covered: typosquats, hostile install scripts, retargeted tags, licence contamination.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-12

Depends on (as written)

HD-01.

What to build

  • cargo deny with a licence allow list. Deny AGPL and GPL, which enforces I2's warning about the forceatlas2 crate and I1's note on Gephi Lite; deny PolyForm, so no GitNexus code arrives through a dependency. cargo audit on advisories. pnpm audit --prod and pnpm licenses list checked against the same list.
  • pnpm: no install scripts except an allow list (onlyBuiltDependencies), and a minimum release age of 7 days for new versions.
  • Dependency updates by pull request (Renovate or Dependabot config in the repo), grouped weekly.
  • SBOM: cargo cyclonedx for the Rust side and @cyclonedx/cyclonedx-npm for the embedded app, merged into one CycloneDX file per release artefact. A THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES file is generated from the same data and embedded in the binary (cartograph --licenses).
  • Vendored fonts and tree-sitter grammars are listed with their licences and source commits.
  • Reproducible build check: two CI builds of the same commit give the same binary hash on Linux. Unspecified as a requirement; proposed as a goal with a nightly report, not a gate, since macOS signing changes bytes.

Done when

adding a GPL crate or a package with a known advisory fails CI; each release carries an SBOM that lists every crate and npm package in the binary.

Security

threats covered: typosquats, hostile install scripts, retargeted tags, licence contamination.

Size

M

Tests

CI steps; a canary pull request with a denied licence.

  • Reconcile the four tables: server plan 1.1 and 4.2 to 4.5, front-end plan 4.2 to 4.5, cross-service plan 4.6, AI plan 4.1 to 4.3.
  • Record every route as read or write. A write route is one of the fixed list in the Interfaces section and nothing else.

IDX-02

Benchmark spike: the three "before code" checks, plus SQLite I/O M

Depends on IDX-01

a benches/ crate with criterion, and a pinned fixture script that clones remotion at 2b1b0c9.

Done when: a results table is committed under docs/research/ with hardware, load average and at least ten runs on a quiet machine (the research's own limit was a busy laptop).

Merge note: Owns every indexer-side benchmark, including the D1 freshness check that SRV-03 also planned to run. The D1 fixture is 30 repos (the decision), with 43 and 50 recorded beside it.

Security
fixtures are cloned public repos; treat them as untrusted (IDX-04's gix open options apply).
Scale
remotion (35,439 commits, 9,498 first-parent, 9,075 files at head); the 60k-file and 200k-link synthetic set.
Efficiency
the research found extraction is 64–78% git object reads through cat-file pipes.
Full text of IDX-02

Depends on (as written)

IDX-01.

What to build

a benches/ crate with criterion, and a pinned fixture script that clones remotion at 2b1b0c9. Four experiments, each writing JSON next to bench.mjs's output so the numbers compare:

  1. B7: gix first-parent walk with tree diff, against git log --raw (the research baseline) and against git2, on remotion's 9,498 first-parent commits. Then rename detection (exact, then 50% similarity with a 1,000-candidate limit) per commit, in time and in agreement with git log -M50%. git2 is a dev-only dependency of the bench crate and never ships.
  2. H1: tree-sitter TS/TSX/JS parse plus an import query over remotion's 103,347 distinct blobs, at 1, 2, 4 and 8 workers. Then oxc_resolver over an in-memory file system (IDX-06) for remotion's 60,039 resolutions. Confirm that resolve_with_context reports file_dependencies and missing_dependencies, and that a custom FileSystem can be supplied. The whole replay design in IDX-06 rests on both.
  3. D1: read HEAD and the indexed-branch tip for 43 repos (the research's multi-repo folder size) cold and warm; count commits behind with a cap.
  4. Research "Next": write 36,193 intervals and a synthetic 200k-link, 60k-file set to SQLite; measure write time, file size, load time and as-of query time from disk.

Done when

a results table is committed under docs/research/ with hardware, load average and at least ten runs on a quiet machine (the research's own limit was a busy laptop). Each of B7, H1 and D1 is marked "holds" or "changed", with the fallback chosen if it changed.

Security

fixtures are cloned public repos; treat them as untrusted (IDX-04's gix open options apply).

Scale

remotion (35,439 commits, 9,498 first-parent, 9,075 files at head); the 60k-file and 200k-link synthetic set.

Efficiency

the research found extraction is 64–78% git object reads through cat-file pipes. In-process gix with memory-mapped packs should remove the pipe cost; this spike measures whether it does.

Size

M.

Tests

the benchmarks themselves; a correctness assert that gix and git log give the same changed paths for every commit.

What to build

a throwaway binary that, against OpenAI, Anthropic (native), Gemini, Ollama and one OpenAI-compatible server (LM Studio or vLLM), checks: streaming text; streaming tool calls with parallel calls; usage in the stream (prompt, completion, cached tokens); reasoning effort per provider; Anthropic cache_control breakpoints; a custom base URL per provider with our own reqwest::Client; cancellation by dropping the stream; whether the default auth resolver reads ambient env vars such as ANTHROPIC_API_KEY. For keyring: set, get and delete on macOS, Windows and Linux with and without Secret Service, and the macOS prompt after the binary is replaced by an upgrade.

Done when

Phase 0 results

measured 22 Nov 2026 · Apple M4, 24 GB, busy machine (load 5 to 40), so times are upper bounds

All nine Phase 0 items are done. No gate failed outright; CI has run locally but not yet on GitHub. Full method, raw results and scripts: docs/research/phase0.md.

GateMeasuredResult
oxc_resolver over files read from git (IDX-02, H1)31,349 resolutions in 51 ms; reports file and missing dependencies, but not the package.json and tsconfig.json it reads (44 of 56,079 answers changed unseen). Keying on those manifests: 0 of 31,703Pass, with an amendment
gix against git log -M50% on remotion's 9,498 first-parent commits (B7)Changed paths agree on every commit. Renames differ on 19 commits (libgit2: 88). Walk: git 555 ms, gix 733 ms, libgit2 11,458 ms; with renames gix 2,584 msPaths pass; renames within 0.2%
Freshness for 30 repos under 100 ms, no network (D1)51 ms on one thread, 36 ms on 8, 12 to 15 ms with a commit-graph; run with every network call deniedPass
sigma 4 at 60k nodes and 200k links (FE-07, I1)p95 frame 16.7 ms with hideEdgesOnMove (33.4 ms without), pick 30.5 ms, load 1.39 s, heap 227 MB, real M4 GPUPass, with hideEdgesOnMove
genai, keyring and ACP (AI-01, G2 to G4)genai 0.7.5 passes every feature on 5 provider shapes against a local mock. keyring 4.2.0 set, get and delete pass, but delete fails after a rebuild. ACP agents keep their own tools unless the vendor switches them offPass; every fail has a fallback
Code2DFD viability (INT-02, H2)17 apps, all Java and Spring. A TypeScript, Go and Python labelled set is started (robot-shop, 21 links)Pass: measure on our own set
Lockfile, licences, CI (HD-01, HD-02, HD-12)npm install refuses and names pnpm; an AGPL crate fails cargo deny; CI covers Linux, macOS and WindowsPass locally; CI not yet run on GitHub

What the results change

  • H1 · IDX-06: each resolution is also keyed on the nearest package.json and tsconfig.json, compared on the fields resolution reads, not their bytes. No candidate-list fallback is needed.
  • B7 · IDX-18: the rename gate becomes "within 1% of commits, differences listed". The rename overhead mark moves from under 2× the walk to under 4× (measured 3.5×, the same as git's own 3.1×).
  • D1 · IDX-15: counting commits behind is the cost, not reading HEAD. Read the commit-graph when present, check repos on a few threads, or answer "behind" first and count afterwards.
  • I1 · FE-08: hideEdgesOnMove is required at file level, and each level's graph stays in memory (rebuilding the 60k level costs 0.9 s). Re-run on an Intel UHD laptop.
  • SRV-04: jumping to a commit from disk takes 5.2 ms median, over the 5 ms mark. Hold the intervals in memory per index generation (23 ms to load, 0.06 ms to scan).
  • G3 · AI-07: always build an explicit provider target so no ambient *_API_KEY is read, and use our own HTTP client with no proxy and no redirects.
  • G4: if a key can't be deleted after a rebuild, overwrite it with an empty value; recheck once releases are signed.
  • H2: precision per confidence level is measured on the hand-labelled TypeScript, Go and Python set; Code2DFD comes later, once Java adapters exist.

Next: Phase 1 can start on the store, the gitoxide reader, the TypeScript extractor and replay (IDX-03 to IDX-07), the query core and graph slice on the generated contract (SRV-02, SRV-04, SRV-07), and moving the app to the generated types (FE-02).

Phase 1 · One repo, indexed, in the browser behind the session token

18 items · 5S 10M 3L · roadmap stage 1, and the first cut of stage 3
Goal

The smallest vertical slice through every layer: index one TypeScript repo into SQLite, read it through the query layer, serve the static export on loopback, and draw the map from the API.

What a user can do at the end

Run cartograph index <repo>, then cartograph open. The browser opens at http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#k=<token>, the token leaves the URL, and the map of that repo's files and import links draws from GET /api/v1/w/{ws}/graph. Moving the date changes which links show. Every other screen still runs on demo getters.

Exit gate
  • The research's six checks pass in Rust on remotion: the tree equals ls-tree, replayed links equal a from-scratch index at 24 sampled commits, and resolutions are about 400k (restated from the research's 60,039: manifests and tsconfigs are keys too), not 37.8 million.
  • Replay equals direct indexing as a property test on generated histories (the first HD-06 property, started here).
  • Requests with a foreign Host, a foreign Origin or no token are refused on every route; the listener isn't reachable on the LAN address (HD-11).
  • No CSP violation on any exported route; curl '/..%2f..%2fetc/passwd' returns 404.
  • The same fixture gives the same golden output on three operating systems; a Rust field change without regenerated types fails CI.
  • Proposed, replaced by IDX-02's numbers: remotion cold under 60 s, warm under 10 s, peak memory under 1 GB; service-level graph under 20 ms.
Risks retired
  • A1 and A2 proven end to end: one binary, static export, no Node at runtime.
  • A3's rules tested from the first request, not retrofitted.
  • The replay method surviving a real parser and disk writes.

SRV-04 and FE-06 ship their first cut here (one at= slice, SVG path) and finish in phase 3. SRV-05's /freshness returns the stored state until SRV-03 lands in phase 2.

IDX-03

Store: schema v1, open rules, pragmas M

Depends on IDX-01

cartograph-store with Index::create, Index::open_read, Index::open_write, and the schema in §4.3.

Done when: a created file reports the application id and user_version; each row of the open-rules table has a test fixture that yields the stated result; sqlite3 index.cartograph .schema matches a golden file.

Security
board G01 lets a user "open an existing index", so an index file is untrusted input.
Scale
the 412 MB example index; 200k links. Page size 4,096 (default) unless IDX-02 part 4 shows 8,192 is faster.
Efficiency
WITHOUT ROWID for small key-value tables; integer keys everywhere; blob ids as 20- or 32-byte BLOB, never hex text; mmap_size of 256 MB for readers (proposed).
Full text of IDX-03

Depends on (as written)

IDX-01.

What to build

cartograph-store with Index::create, Index::open_read, Index::open_write, and the schema in §4.3.

  • PRAGMA application_id = 0x43415254 ("CART", proposed value) and user_version = 1.
  • On every connection: journal_mode = WAL, synchronous = NORMAL, foreign_keys = ON, trusted_schema = OFF, busy_timeout = 5000; SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE on; extension loading off. Readers add query_only = ON.
  • Open rules (B3):
FileResult
application_id wrongError "not a Cartograph index"
format_version equals this binary'sOpen
Newer, and min_reader_version ≤ this binary's formatOpen read-only, state current with a warning
Newer, and min_reader_version greaterError naming the upgrade, exit 1
OlderState needs-migrate; read-only if readable; IDX-16 migrates or rebuilds
Truncated or failing PRAGMA quick_checkError "file damaged", action index --rebuild (board G02 errors)
  • Tables are STRICT. All statements are prepared and cached.

Done when

a created file reports the application id and user_version; each row of the open-rules table has a test fixture that yields the stated result; sqlite3 index.cartograph .schema matches a golden file.

Security

board G01 lets a user "open an existing index", so an index file is untrusted input. Mitigations: the application-id check, trusted_schema = OFF, defensive mode, no extensions, no triggers or views in the schema, quick_check before first use, and parameters instead of string-built SQL everywhere. The data folder is created 0700 and files 0600.

Scale

the 412 MB example index; 200k links. Page size 4,096 (default) unless IDX-02 part 4 shows 8,192 is faster.

Efficiency

WITHOUT ROWID for small key-value tables; integer keys everywhere; blob ids as 20- or 32-byte BLOB, never hex text; mmap_size of 256 MB for readers (proposed).

Size

M.

Tests

golden schema; unit tests per open rule; a fuzz target that opens arbitrary bytes as an index and must return an error, never panic.

IDX-04

Git reading with gitoxide: walk, diff, blobs M

Depends on IDX-01 IDX-02

cartograph-git

Done when: on remotion at 2b1b0c9 the walk yields 9,498 steps, and the tree rebuilt from the steps equals git ls-tree -r at head and at 24 sampled commits (the research's own check).

Security
a repo is untrusted content. Never run repo-configured programs.
Scale
9,498 first-parent commits and 105,293 changed-path events on remotion; 35k-commit histories; 43 repos walked in parallel.
Efficiency
one walk per repo, repos walked in parallel with rayon. Path filtering (tracked extensions, files.exclude, scope prefixes) happens inside the diff callback, so skipped subtrees are…
Full text of IDX-04

Depends on (as written)

IDX-01, IDX-02 (B7 result).

What to build

cartograph-git:

  • Repo::open(path) with gix::open::Options::isolated()-style options: no hooks, no fsmonitor, no external diff or filter drivers, no credential helpers.
  • walk_first_parent(from: Option<Oid>, to: Oid) -> impl Iterator<Item = CommitStep>, oldest first. Each step has the sha, the true committer time, the corrected time (max(ct, prev + 1), as in bench.mjs), the author name, the summary line, and the changed entries (path, old oid, new oid, mode) from a tree diff against the first parent only. Merge commits are diffed against their first parent; the research notes the replayed tree drifts otherwise.
  • Only regular files (mode 100644, 100755) count. Symlinks and gitlinks count as absent, as in the research.
  • read_blob(oid, max_bytes) reads the header first and returns TooLarge without reading the body past the cap.
  • A thread-local repository handle per worker (to_thread_local()), with an object cache.
  • Shallow detection (.git/shallow), bare detection, SHA-1 and SHA-256 object formats (Oid holds either).
  • Fallback to the git command only for partial clones (B7), behind the same trait (§4.1 ObjectSource).

Done when

on remotion at 2b1b0c9 the walk yields 9,498 steps, and the tree rebuilt from the steps equals git ls-tree -r at head and at 24 sampled commits (the research's own check).

Security

a repo is untrusted content. Never run repo-configured programs. Tree paths with .. segments, absolute paths, NUL bytes or (on Windows) reserved names are kept as opaque strings and never joined to a real directory; the indexer reads the object database only, never the checkout (the working tree is read only in IDX-24, under the repo root after canonicalisation). The blob size cap prevents memory exhaustion. A commit with more than 50,000 changed paths (proposed) is processed in streamed chunks.

Scale

9,498 first-parent commits and 105,293 changed-path events on remotion; 35k-commit histories; 43 repos walked in parallel.

Efficiency

one walk per repo, repos walked in parallel with rayon. Path filtering (tracked extensions, files.exclude, scope prefixes) happens inside the diff callback, so skipped subtrees are never descended. Paths are interned.

Size

M.

Tests

unit tests on a hand-made history (the bench.mjs self-check history, ported); property test that replaying steps reproduces ls-tree on random generated histories with merges; golden step list for a small fixture repo; a fuzz target on a corrupt pack or loose object.

IDX-05

Fact schema, the TS/JS extractor, and the blob-id cache M

Depends on IDX-01 IDX-02

cartograph-parse::Fact and the Extractor trait (§4.1, §4.5).

Done when: on remotion, the set of import specifiers per blob is a superset of bench.mjs's regular-expression output minus its known false matches in strings and comments (a diff report lists every difference, and each difference class is…

Security
parser input is hostile. Size cap, time limit, and a recursion-safe grammar (tree-sitter is iterative, but the query walk is depth-limited to 2,000, proposed).
Scale
103,347 distinct blobs on remotion; the research's 61%-from-cache example on re-index.
Efficiency
extraction runs on a rayon pool; the worker count defaults to min(cores, 8) and is settled by IDX-02, because the research could not settle it (best was 4, 2, 8 and 2 in four…
Full text of IDX-05

Depends on (as written)

IDX-01, IDX-02 (H1 result).

What to build

  • cartograph-parse::Fact and the Extractor trait (§4.1, §4.5). The envelope matches H4's NDJSON, so subprocess adapters and built-in extractors produce one shape.
  • Extractor ts-imports (version 1.0.0) on tree-sitter-typescript (ts, tsx) and tree-sitter-javascript (js, jsx, mjs, cjs). It emits import facts for static imports, re-exports, require("…") and import("…") with a literal argument, each with a span and a style (esm, reexport, cjs, dynamic, type-only). A non-literal argument emits import.dynamic-unresolved for coverage.
  • Extractor manifest-js (version 1.0.0) for package.json (name, exports, imports, main, workspaces, dependency names) and tsconfig*.json or jsconfig.json (baseUrl, paths, extends, references). These are resolver inputs as well as facts.
  • Fact cache at <data-root>/cache/facts.db (§4.4), keyed by (blob oid, extractor, version). One file shared by all workspaces (R2).
  • Skip rules: over 1 MB, a NUL byte in the first 8 KB (binary), an LFS pointer header, or a generated-file banner. Each yields a skipped record with the reason instead of facts.
  • A parse time limit per blob (tree-sitter cancellation flag, 2 s, proposed).

Done when

on remotion, the set of import specifiers per blob is a superset of bench.mjs's regular-expression output minus its known false matches in strings and comments (a diff report lists every difference, and each difference class is explained); a second run reads 100% from the cache and parses nothing.

Security

parser input is hostile. Size cap, time limit, and a recursion-safe grammar (tree-sitter is iterative, but the query walk is depth-limited to 2,000, proposed). Grammars are vendored at pinned commits and compiled from C in build.rs; no grammar is downloaded at run time. Facts hold specifier strings and spans only, never source text beyond the specifier, so the cache can't leak file bodies. Specifier strings are untrusted text (F8) and are flagged as such in the schema.

Scale

103,347 distinct blobs on remotion; the research's 61%-from-cache example on re-index.

Efficiency

extraction runs on a rayon pool; the worker count defaults to min(cores, 8) and is settled by IDX-02, because the research could not settle it (best was 4, 2, 8 and 2 in four sweeps). One parser and one compiled query per thread, reused. Cache writes are batched in one transaction per 1,000 blobs. Facts are encoded with postcard, sorted, so the encoding is deterministic for IDX-17.

Size

M.

Tests

unit tests per import form; golden facts for a fixture folder; a fuzz target (cargo fuzz) feeding arbitrary bytes to each extractor; a property test that the same bytes always give the same encoded facts.

IDX-06

Resolution and the replay engine L

Depends on IDX-04 IDX-05

cartograph-facts::Replay, the production form of bench.mjs makeIndex()

Done when: the research's six checks pass in Rust on remotion: tree equals ls-tree at sampled commits; replayed links equal a from-scratch index at 24 sampled commits; written intervals reproduce the link set at each sampled step; the…

Security
ReplayFs is the mitigation for path traversal through config: a tsconfig extends of ../../../../etc/x or a paths entry pointing outside the repo resolves inside the virtual…
Scale
remotion: 36,193 intervals from 60,039 resolutions. Target map: 60k files and 200k links live at once.
Efficiency
integer ids for paths and keys; waiting as a hash map of small vectors; no allocation per step in the steady state.
Full text of IDX-06

Depends on (as written)

IDX-04, IDX-05.

What to build

cartograph-facts::Replay, the production form of bench.mjs makeIndex():

  • State: tree: path → blob oid, refs: (file, fact) → resolution, waiting: key → set of refs (the reverse index), links: (kind, src, dst) → count, open_at: link → seq.
  • apply(step): update the tree; drop the refs of removed or changed blobs; add the refs of new blobs; collect every key whose existence or content changed; re-resolve only the refs waiting on those keys; when a link's count leaves zero open an interval, when it returns to zero close it; a link that closes and reopens within one step is left open.
  • The Resolver trait (§4.1) returns a target plus the set of keys the answer depended on. Two kinds of key: an existence key (a path that was probed and found or missing) and a content key (a config file whose bytes were read: tsconfig.json, package.json).
  • TsResolver wraps oxc_resolver over a ReplayFs: a virtual file system backed by the replay tree and the fact cache, so it sees the tree at that commit and never the disk. file_dependencies and missing_dependencies from resolve_with_context become the keys. Covers tsconfig paths and extends, exports and imports maps, and the TS ESM rule (./x.js names x.ts). node_modules is not in history, so bare specifiers resolve through a package key (#name) to the workspace package.json declaring that name, as in the research; a bare specifier with no such package is an external dependency and is recorded as a package.dep edge target, not as unresolved.
  • If IDX-02 shows oxc_resolver can't be driven this way, the fallback is the research's ordered candidate list plus a hand-written paths and exports matcher.
  • Output: a stream of IntervalEvent::{Open, Close} and UnresolvedEvents to the store (IDX-07, IDX-22).

Done when

the research's six checks pass in Rust on remotion: tree equals ls-tree at sampled commits; replayed links equal a from-scratch index at 24 sampled commits; written intervals reproduce the link set at each sampled step; the replay without the reverse index writes identical intervals; an incremental run over the last 100 commits ends in the same state; and the resolution count is of the order of 60,039, not 37.8 million.

Security

ReplayFs is the mitigation for path traversal through config: a tsconfig extends of ../../../../etc/x or a paths entry pointing outside the repo resolves inside the virtual tree or not at all. extends chains are depth-limited (32) and cycle-checked. Symlinks are absent from the tree, so there is no symlink escape. A commit that touches a hot key (a root tsconfig.json that every ref waits on) re-resolves many refs; that is bounded by the tree size and logged when it exceeds 100,000.

Scale

remotion: 36,193 intervals from 60,039 resolutions. Target map: 60k files and 200k links live at once.

Efficiency

integer ids for paths and keys; waiting as a hash map of small vectors; no allocation per step in the steady state. The research's replay is independent of tree size; keep that property and add a debug counter that fails a test if any step iterates the whole tree. Resolver results are memoised per (directory, specifier, config generation).

Size

L.

Tests

unit tests for every resolution rule; the six research checks as an integration test on a pinned small repo in CI, and on remotion in a nightly job; a property test comparing replay with from-scratch on random histories (random adds, deletes, moves, config edits); a differential test of the reverse index against the no-reverse-index ablation.

What to build

cartograph-index::build_single(repo, out), wired to a temporary cartograph index <dir> command. It writes repo, commits, file, file_path, file_version, fact, node (file nodes only), link, interval and run. One transaction per 1,000 commits. No registry, no manifest, no lock yet.

Done when

this is the roadmap's stage 1 gate. One run over a real TS/JS repo produces one SQLite file in which every import link has the commit that opened it and, where removed, the commit that closed it. A query links as of <sha> on the file equals the from-scratch index at that commit.

Security

Scale

remotion end to end. Proposed targets, to be replaced by IDX-02 numbers: cold cache under 60 s, warm cache under 10 s on an 8-core laptop, peak memory under 1 GB.

Efficiency

walk, extract and replay are a pipeline: walk streams steps into a bounded channel, extraction runs ahead on distinct new blob ids, and replay consumes in order. Prepared inserts; intervals are written when they close or at batch end.

Size

Tests

SRV-02

Query core: reader pool, snapshots, generation, time and ids M

Depends on IDX-03 IDX-07

Store::open(workspace_id) -> Store. A pool of read-only connections (SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY, PRAGMA query_only=ON, mmap_size=256MB, cache_size=-16000).

Done when: 8 reader threads run queries during a writer batch with no SQLITE_BUSY, and each sees one consistent generation.

Merge note: Owns cartograph-query. Needs from IDX: meta.generation bumped on every committed batch (IDX-13), not only per rebuild as IDX R17 proposed.

Security
All SQL is static with bound parameters. No string-built SQL from request input.
Scale
the 412 MB index; 60k files and 200k links. A long reader stops the WAL from being truncated (B2), so no snapshot may outlive one request: 5 s deadline by default, 30 s hard cap.
Efficiency
prepare_cached on each connection. Queries run on spawn_blocking. mmap shares the OS page cache across the app, CLI and MCP processes.
Full text of SRV-02

Depends on (as written)

IDX (index schema, meta table, data-directory paths, registry).

What to build

  • Store::open(workspace_id) -> Store. A pool of read-only connections (SQLITE_OPEN_READONLY, PRAGMA query_only=ON, mmap_size=256MB, cache_size=-16000). Pool size is min(8, cores).
  • Format gate from B3: check application_id, user_version and min_reader_version. Return IndexState::NeedsMigrate, or a "too new" error.
  • Snapshot: one read transaction. It carries generation (a counter in meta, bumped by every committed writer batch; IDX must provide it) and fact_hash.
  • At: parses now, an ISO date, YYYY-MM, or a commit sha, and resolves it to a timeline sequence number. Intervals are [valid_from, valid_to), as in the research note.
  • NodeRef: accepts an opaque id ([A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}), a repo:path, or a unique name. Ambiguous refs return candidates, not a guess.
  • Limits { max_rows, max_bytes, deadline }, enforced with sqlite3_progress_handler and an InterruptHandle.
  • QueryError with stable codes (see 4.3).
  • Swap detection: PRAGMA data_version plus an inode check on index.cartograph. Reopen the pool after a rebuild renames a new file into place (B2).

Done when

8 reader threads run queries during a writer batch with no SQLITE_BUSY, and each sees one consistent generation. A query past its deadline returns timeout within 50 ms of the limit. A rebuilt file is picked up without a restart.

Security

  • All SQL is static with bound parameters. No string-built SQL from request input.
  • Read-only handles mean a query-layer bug can't change the index.
  • Ids are validated before any lookup.

Scale

the 412 MB index; 60k files and 200k links. A long reader stops the WAL from being truncated (B2), so no snapshot may outlive one request: 5 s deadline by default, 30 s hard cap.

Efficiency

prepare_cached on each connection. Queries run on spawn_blocking. mmap shares the OS page cache across the app, CLI and MCP processes.

Size

M.

Tests

unit (At parsing, ref resolution), property (interval membership matches a brute-force scan), concurrency test with a writer thread, fault injection (file swapped mid-request).

SRV-04

Graph slice: time and level of detail L

Depends on SRV-02 INT-01

graph(GraphQuery) -> GraphSlice.

Done when: the result equals a brute-force reference on a fixture at 24 sampled commits (the research note's check).

Merge note: Owns the GraphSlice shape (section 4.4 of the server plan), which replaces FE's 4.5. In range mode links.from and links.to are timeline sequence numbers; the browser maps dates to them with the commit index it already loads (FE-09). First cut in phase 1, roll-ups, range mode and stored positions in phase 3.

Security
a response cap of 100k nodes and 400k links; above it the request fails with too_large and names a narrower scope.
Scale
60k files and 200k links. The research note scanned 36,193 intervals in 0.06 ms, so a plain scan with an index on valid_from is enough; no snapshot anchors.
Efficiency
roll-ups are computed once per (generation, lod, scope, filters) and held in a 64 MB LRU. The response is streamed from the cursor, not built in memory first.
Full text of SRV-04

Depends on (as written)

SRV-02, IDX (node and edge tables, stored layout from I2).

What to build

graph(GraphQuery) -> GraphSlice.

  • lod: domain, service, module, file or symbol. scope: a node whose subtree is expanded one level further.
  • Edges are rolled up to the visible ancestors: one row per (source, target, kind) with a count, the minimum and maximum confidence, and a cross-service flag.
  • Two time modes:
    • at=<t>: one slice. Used by MCP, the CLI and the table view (I3).
    • range=<from>..<to>: each link carries its intervals, or for rolled-up links a per-day step series. The browser then scrubs without a request per frame, as isActive does today.
  • Filters: kinds, min_confidence (default hides scores under 0.30, H2), owner, repo.
  • Positions come from IDX's stored layout; the query layer never computes layout.
  • A columnar encoding for large slices (see 4.4).

Done when

the result equals a brute-force reference on a fixture at 24 sampled commits (the research note's check). The service level of a 62-service workspace returns in under 20 ms. A file-level slice of 60k nodes and 200k links streams in under 500 ms cold and under 50 ms from cache.

Security

a response cap of 100k nodes and 400k links; above it the request fails with too_large and names a narrower scope.

Scale

60k files and 200k links. The research note scanned 36,193 intervals in 0.06 ms, so a plain scan with an index on valid_from is enough; no snapshot anchors.

Efficiency

roll-ups are computed once per (generation, lod, scope, filters) and held in a 64 MB LRU. The response is streamed from the cursor, not built in memory first.

Size

L.

Tests

property (roll-up counts equal the sum of leaf links), golden slices, a 60k/200k synthetic fixture shared with the I1 benchmark, criterion benchmarks as CI gates.

SRV-05

cartograph open: the locked-down server M

Depends on SRV-01 SRV-02 SRV-04

axum on tokio. Bind 127.0.0.1:0. --port is allowed; a non-loopback --host is refused with a reason.

Done when: these all pass as automated tests

Merge note: Token ruling: fragment, then sessionStorage, then Authorization: Bearer. No cookie and no POST /api/session. Port ruling: reuse the last port from server.json when free. Dev loop: FE-01's --dev-ui flag in debug builds, not a Next rewrite.

Security
see section 5 for the full threat table. In short
Scale
64 concurrent requests, 16 SSE streams.
Efficiency
no compression on loopback; it costs CPU and saves nothing. HTTP/1.1 keep-alive only.
Full text of SRV-05

Depends on (as written)

SRV-01 to SRV-04.

What to build

  • axum on tokio. Bind 127.0.0.1:0. --port is allowed; a non-loopback --host is refused with a reason.
  • Token: 32 random bytes from the OS, base64url. The browser opens http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#k=<token>. A fragment is never sent to a server or in a Referer.
  • The app's boot script moves k into storage for that origin and removes it from the URL. Every API call sends Authorization: Bearer <token>. Compared in constant time.
  • Middleware, in this order: Host allowlist (127.0.0.1:<port>, localhost:<port>, [::1]:<port>), Origin allowlist (absent or exactly this origin), Sec-Fetch-Site not cross-site, bearer token on /api/*, body limit 1 MB, a 30 s timeout, a concurrency limit of 64.
  • No CORS layer at all. OPTIONS returns 405.
  • First routes: /api/v1/meta, /api/version, /workspaces, /freshness, /repos, /graph.
  • server.json in the data directory (0600): pid, port, token, binary version. A second cartograph open reuses the running server and only opens a browser tab.
  • Opens the browser with the open crate; --no-browser prints the URL instead.
  • Graceful shutdown on SIGINT; removes server.json.

Done when

these all pass as automated tests:

  • A request with Host: evil.example gets 403.
  • A request with a foreign Origin gets 403.
  • An API request without the token gets 401.
  • A cross-origin fetch from another local port can't read any response.
  • The listener is not reachable on the machine's LAN address.

Security

see section 5 for the full threat table. In short:

  • DNS rebinding: Host allowlist.
  • CSRF: a bearer header is required, which a cross-site form or image can't send, and no CORS means a preflight fails.
  • Other local users: the token.
  • Same-user processes can read server.json. This is accepted; they can already read the index.
  • Static assets need no token (they are the same bytes for everyone) but still pass the Host check.

Scale

64 concurrent requests, 16 SSE streams.

Efficiency

no compression on loopback; it costs CPU and saves nothing. HTTP/1.1 keep-alive only.

Size

M.

Tests

integration tests with a real listener and raw requests for each rejected case; a rebinding simulation (correct IP, wrong Host); a fuzz target on header parsing.

SRV-07

API conventions: envelope, errors, pagination, caching, types M

Depends on SRV-05 INT-01

One envelope and one error shape (4.2, 4.3) shared by HTTP, --json and MCP structuredContent.

Done when: a contract test asserts that every route's body validates against the generated schema.

Merge note: Owns the HTTP route table agreed in INT-01. Types: schemars JSON Schema is the one source; lib/api/types.gen.ts is generated from it (FE-02), so ts-rs is not needed. Default page size 50, maximum 1,000.

Security
error bodies never contain SQL, absolute paths outside the workspace, or stack traces.
Scale
list endpoints stay O(page), not O(table).
Efficiency
serialise straight to the socket with serde_json::to_writer over a body stream for large lists.
Full text of SRV-07

Depends on (as written)

SRV-05.

What to build

  • One envelope and one error shape (4.2, 4.3) shared by HTTP, --json and MCP structuredContent.
  • Cursors: opaque base64url of {sort key, filter hash, generation}. Keyset paging, so a page is stable while the index changes. A cursor used with different filters returns bad_cursor.
  • Limits: default 100, maximum 1,000 on HTTP; default 50 on MCP (F2).
  • ETag: "<ws>.<generation>.<schema>" with Cache-Control: private, no-cache. If-None-Match is answered with 304 before any SQL runs. /freshness, /events and /ask are no-store.
  • Response cache: an LRU of serialised bodies keyed by (generation, route, canonical query), 64 MB, emptied when the generation changes.
  • Rust types derive serde, schemars and ts-rs. A build step writes lib/model.gen.ts and an OpenAPI file, and CI fails if they are out of date.
  • Cartograph-Generation and Cartograph-State response headers.

Done when

a contract test asserts that every route's body validates against the generated schema. A second identical request returns 304 in under 1 ms. A sync bumps the generation and the next request returns 200.

Security

error bodies never contain SQL, absolute paths outside the workspace, or stack traces. Cursors are validated, and their contents are never trusted as SQL.

Scale

list endpoints stay O(page), not O(table).

Efficiency

serialise straight to the socket with serde_json::to_writer over a body stream for large lists.

Size

M.

Tests

contract tests, a property test for cursor round-trips, golden errors.

FE-03

API client, cache and session M

Depends on FE-01 FE-02 SRV-05

lib/api/ with

Done when: with the binary running on a fixture index, /coverage renders from GET /api/w/:ws/coverage; reloading with the token stripped still works; a second tab opened without a token gets the "open from the CLI" screen, not data; a…

Merge note: The text below proposes a cookie and an X-Cartograph header. Ruling: bearer header from sessionStorage, no cookie, no session exchange; the bearer header already forces a preflight. Generation header is Cartograph-Generation.

Security
the token never goes into localStorage, logs, error text or share links.
Scale
cache capped at 64 MB or 500 entries, least recently used first.
Efficiency
ETag and If-None-Match for anything not generation-keyed. One request per resource per generation.
Full text of FE-03

Depends on (as written)

FE-01, SRV-05.

What to build

lib/api/ with:

  • client.ts: get<T>(route, params, {signal}) and send<T>(method, route, body). One error type carrying the server's {code, what, why, try} (the E1 error shape). Every mutating call sends X-Cartograph: 1.
  • session.ts: on first load, read the token from the URL, exchange it with POST /api/session, then strip it with history.replaceState. The token transport is unspecified beyond "the browser URL carries a random token" (A3). Proposed: the Jupyter pattern, token in the URL once, then an HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/ cookie.
  • cache.ts and useResource(route, params): an in-memory map keyed by (workspace, generation, route, params), in-flight de-duplication, abort on unmount, and stale-while-revalidate to match D1. About 150 lines, no dependency. generation comes from the X-Cartograph-Generation response header and from the index.changed event. Responses for a past generation are immutable, so they never revalidate.
  • lib/source.ts keeps its function names and becomes async hooks (useWorkspaces, useFreshness, …) so the importing files change mechanically.

Done when

with the binary running on a fixture index, /coverage renders from GET /api/w/:ws/coverage; reloading with the token stripped still works; a second tab opened without a token gets the "open from the CLI" screen, not data; a 401 clears state and shows that screen.

Security

the token never goes into localStorage, logs, error text or share links. Stripping it from the URL keeps it out of history and out of Referer. The custom header forces a preflight on cross-origin writes, which the server refuses (CSRF). No Access-Control-Allow-Origin is ever expected; the client treats one as a bug.

Scale

cache capped at 64 MB or 500 entries, least recently used first. Graph payloads are stored as typed arrays (FE-07).

Efficiency

ETag and If-None-Match for anything not generation-keyed. One request per resource per generation. Search requests are debounced 120 ms and aborted when superseded.

Size

M

Tests

unit tests on cache keys, de-duplication and abort; contract tests against recorded fixtures; Playwright for the token exchange and the 401 path.

HD-11

Local-server and privacy test suite M

Depends on SRV-05 FE-03

black-box tests against the running binary

Done when: all checks run per pull request; the egress test has failed at least once on a deliberate violation to prove it works.

Merge note: Token checks test the bearer header, not a cookie. Also the acceptance test for the fixed list of write routes.

Security
this item is the security acceptance test for A3, F4, F8, G4 and G6.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-11

Depends on (as written)

SRV-05, FE-03.

What to build

black-box tests against the running binary:

  • Bind: refuses 0.0.0.0 and any non-loopback address; IPv6 ::1 behaves the same.
  • DNS rebinding: a request with Host: evil.example:port is refused. Allowed hosts are exactly 127.0.0.1:port and localhost:port.
  • Origin: any Origin other than its own is refused, on every method, including the SSE and Ask streams.
  • Token: no token or cookie gives 401 on every /api route and no data in the body; the token is compared in constant time; it changes each start.
  • CSRF: a cross-site form post and a no-preflight fetch both fail.
  • Static handler: .., encoded traversal, absolute paths, NUL bytes and symlinks all return 404.
  • Headers: CSP from FE-01, X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin, frame-ancestors 'none'.
  • Egress: the whole e2e suite runs in a network namespace that allows only loopback and a mock provider. Any other connection attempt fails the build. A second run with no model set must make zero outbound connections. This is the executable form of "local only".
  • Secrets: grep the data folder, logs, ask.db, API responses and crash reports for a planted canary key after a full run.
  • Prompt injection: a fixture repo whose comments, README and commit messages carry instructions. Assert that no tool description, server instruction, CARTOGRAPH.md or instruction block contains repo text (F8), that Ask's answer renders no link the citations didn't produce (G6), and that MCP returns such text only in untrusted_text fields.
  • Resource limits: request body cap, concurrent stream cap, slow-loris timeout, query time limit.

Done when

all checks run per pull request; the egress test has failed at least once on a deliberate violation to prove it works.

Security

this item is the security acceptance test for A3, F4, F8, G4 and G6.

Size

M

Tests

itself.

Phase 2 · A real workspace that stays current

20 items · 5S 12M 3L · roadmap stage 2, first half
Goal

Discovery, the manifest, the registry and the data folder; many repos on one timeline; one writer; incremental sync; the freshness check on every read; and the app's shell, live events and add-workspace wizard on top.

What a user can do at the end

Run cartograph init in a folder with a .code-workspace file, or add the workspace from the app without the terminal. Commit in any repo: the header chip moves to syncing and back with no reload. cartograph status, the app and CARTOGRAPH.md report the same commits behind.

Exit gate
  • Freshness under 100 ms for 30 repos (D1); ten concurrent sync requests give exactly one writer and nine exits with code 0 inside 50 ms (D2).
  • SIGKILL at 100 random points never leaves an index that fails quick_check; the next sync ends with the same fact hash as an uninterrupted run.
  • Incremental equals full: any split of a history into sync runs gives one fact hash. 100 new commits on remotion under 500 ms warm (proposed; 105 ms measured in memory).
  • 43 repositories and 14,730 commits on one timeline within 8.0 s (measured budget).
  • A fresh init leaves no group- or world-readable file in the data folder.
  • Performance gates run on every pull request from here on (HD-09).
Risks retired
  • WAL, locks and atomic rename on Windows.
  • localStorage loss on a new port: preferences move to the server here.
  • Cross-repo ordering by corrected date.
  • A background sync that forks without limit.

SRV-14 lands route by route: discovery, workspaces, config and preferences here; corrections with XS-13 in phase 4.

IDX-08

Discovery: workspace file, git checks, scopes M

Depends on IDX-01 IDX-04

cartograph-discovery

Done when: the spec's acme.code-workspace example yields exactly the spec's result table (two repos plain, platform as one repo with two scopes, the absolute path, the remote URI skipped, notes skipped), verified by a fixture test.

Security
the workspace file can name any path, which is the user's choice, but discovery only reads git metadata there and never follows symlinks while walking.
Scale
43 repos (research), 62 services across 9–14 repos (boards). Discovery under 500 ms for 50 folders (proposed).
Efficiency
one discover per folder, cached by common dir.
Full text of IDX-08

Depends on (as written)

IDX-01, IDX-04.

What to build

cartograph-discovery:

  • find_workspace_files(dir): the folder and two levels below; skips node_modules, .git, hidden folders.
  • parse_workspace(file) with jsonc-parser; reads folders[] (path, uri, name), top-level remoteAuthority, and settings["files.exclude"] (keys with value true only; search.exclude ignored).
  • Folder resolution per C1 and inventory row 3. Backslashes in path are accepted. Duplicates are removed after canonicalisation; on a case-insensitive file system the comparison ignores case.
  • Git check per folder: the work-tree top level through gix::discover; a folder below the top level becomes a scope prefix; folders are grouped into repos by common dir, so worktrees of one repo are one repo; shallow flagged; bare skipped; a submodule is its own repo only when the workspace file lists it.
  • Output: Resolution { repos: Vec<ResolvedRepo>, skipped: Vec<Skipped{folder, reason}> }, where reason is one of a closed enum (not-a-git-repository, not-on-this-machine, bare-repository, missing, remote-workspace, duplicate). This is the table on board S01 and in spec §1.
  • doctor inputs: literal ~ or ${env:…} in a path; shallow repos.

Done when

the spec's acme.code-workspace example yields exactly the spec's result table (two repos plain, platform as one repo with two scopes, the absolute path, the remote URI skipped, notes skipped), verified by a fixture test.

Security

the workspace file can name any path, which is the user's choice, but discovery only reads git metadata there and never follows symlinks while walking. uri values other than file: are never opened. Path strings are canonicalised before use and before comparison, so two spellings can't index one repo twice. The JSONC input is size-capped at 5 MB (proposed).

Scale

43 repos (research), 62 services across 9–14 repos (boards). Discovery under 500 ms for 50 folders (proposed).

Efficiency

one discover per folder, cached by common dir.

Size

M.

Tests

table-driven unit tests for each folder rule; fixtures with a worktree, a submodule, a shallow clone and a bare repo; a property test that folder order doesn't change the result; a fuzz target on the JSONC reader.
