IDX-10

cartograph.yaml: types, schema, validation M

Depends on IDX-08 IDX-09

Rust types in cartograph-discovery::manifest with serde and schemars (§4.6); cartograph.schema.json generated at build time, shipped in the binary and written next to the data folder so the # yaml-language-server…

Done when: a manifest written by init validates against the generated schema in a third-party validator; version: 2 is refused with an upgrade message; an unknown key is a warning, not an error (proposed, so older binaries tolerate…

Security
the manifest is committed, so a teammate's manifest is untrusted input.
Scale
62 services, 14 repos.
Efficiency
parsed once per command.
Full text of IDX-10

Depends on (as written)

IDX-08, IDX-09.

What to build

Rust types in cartograph-discovery::manifest with serde and schemars (§4.6); cartograph.schema.json generated at build time, shipped in the binary and written next to the data folder so the # yaml-language-server: $schema=… comment resolves offline. Read, validate, write (stable key order, comments at the top only). Validation errors carry the YAML line and column and a fix.

  • Sections owned here: version, workspace, repos[], options, services[] (shape only; detection is IDX-20). rules[] and baseline are parsed and passed through to the rules owner (H6).
  • A config_hash over the parts that change facts (repos, branches, scopes, excludes, size cap, extractor options), used by IDX-17 and IDX-21.
  • A YAML parser that is maintained (serde_yaml is archived; proposed serde_yaml_ng or saphyr), with alias expansion limited.

Done when

a manifest written by init validates against the generated schema in a third-party validator; version: 2 is refused with an upgrade message; an unknown key is a warning, not an error (proposed, so older binaries tolerate additive changes); two machines with the same manifest and commits get the same config_hash.

Security

the manifest is committed, so a teammate's manifest is untrusted input. No field is ever executed or expanded. path fields are relative to the manifest and may use ../ (C5); they are canonicalised and then shown to the user by init before anything is indexed. index_path is honoured only from user config, never from the committed manifest, so a repo can't redirect writes (proposed; the decisions say only "in config"). YAML alias bombs are bounded by a 1 MB input cap and an expansion limit.

Scale

62 services, 14 repos.

Efficiency

parsed once per command.

Size

M.

Tests

golden schema file; round-trip property test (parse, write, parse gives equal values); negative fixtures for each validation error; a fuzz target on the YAML reader.

IDX-11

Data folder, workspace id, registry M

Depends on IDX-08 IDX-10

Data folder per B1: ~/.cartograph/workspaces/<id>/ on every platform (CARTOGRAPH_HOME moves it; an explicitly set XDG_DATA_HOME is honoured on Linux; revised 22 Nov 2026) (proposed; the decisions name only %APPDATA% for the registry).

Done when: init in one repo, then any command run from a subfolder of any repo in the workspace, finds the same index; moving a clone and running doctor --fix re-links it; an index_path inside a Dropbox folder is refused with the…

Security
registry and data folder are user-only (0600, 0700). The registry is the allow-list for writes
Scale
tens of workspaces, hundreds of repos; registry read under 5 ms.
Efficiency
the registry is small TOML read whole; lookups by a sorted path prefix match.
Full text of IDX-11

Depends on (as written)

IDX-08, IDX-10.

What to build

  • Data folder per B1: ~/.cartograph/workspaces/<id>/ on every platform (CARTOGRAPH_HOME moves it; an explicitly set XDG_DATA_HOME is honoured on Linux; revised 22 Nov 2026) (proposed; the decisions name only %APPDATA% for the registry). Layout in §4.2.
  • Workspace id: 8 hex characters. Proposed: random at first init, stored in the registry and in meta, so moving the workspace folder keeps its index. The spec's example is 7f3a91c2 and gives no rule.
  • Time Machine exclusion through the com.apple.metadata:com_apple_backup_excludeItem extended attribute.
  • Synced-folder refusal for index_path: iCloud Drive (~/Library/Mobile Documents, and Desktop or Documents when iCloud manages them), Dropbox, OneDrive, Google Drive, detected from known roots and from ~/Library/CloudStorage/*; on Windows the OneDrive environment variables.
  • Registry ~/.cartograph/registry.toml (§4.7): written under an fd-lock on registry.lock, to a temp file, fsync, rename. Registry::workspace_for(path) walks up from a path to a repo root and returns its workspace, which is how cartograph mcp runs with no path. Re-matching a moved clone by remote URL plus root commit. A warning when two paths share remote URL and root commit.

Done when

init in one repo, then any command run from a subfolder of any repo in the workspace, finds the same index; moving a clone and running doctor --fix re-links it; an index_path inside a Dropbox folder is refused with the reason; two concurrent inits leave a valid registry.

Security

registry and data folder are user-only (0600, 0700). The registry is the allow-list for writes: sync acts only on repos in it (F8). Registry entries are validated on read (paths must be absolute and canonical; an entry whose path now resolves somewhere else through a symlink is ignored with a warning). The remote URL is stored with credentials stripped (https://user:token@host/… becomes https://host/…).

Scale

tens of workspaces, hundreds of repos; registry read under 5 ms.

Efficiency

the registry is small TOML read whole; lookups by a sorted path prefix match.

Size

M.

Tests

unit tests for id stability and path matching; a concurrency test with 20 writers; fixtures for each synced-folder root; a test that a URL with embedded credentials is stripped.

IDX-12

Merged multi-repo timeline and cross-repo package links M

Depends on IDX-07 IDX-08 IDX-11

a k-way merge of per-repo step streams by corrected date, tie-broken by (repo id, position), giving each step a dense global seq.

Done when: on a folder of repos the Rust result equals bench.mjs's for the same input (the research: 43 repos, 14,730 commits, 153 cross-repo links dated), and every interval's from_seq commit belongs to the repo whose change caused it.

Security
a package name is attacker-chosen text in a repo; two repos declaring the same name is a real case (forks, vendored copies).
Scale
43 repos and 14,730 commits in 8.0 s in the research; 17,207 commits across 4 repos in the spec's example.
Efficiency
per-repo walks in parallel feeding the merge; extraction is shared, because blob ids dedupe across repos (forks and vendored copies parse once).
Full text of IDX-12

Depends on (as written)

IDX-07, IDX-08, IDX-11.

What to build

a k-way merge of per-repo step streams by corrected date, tie-broken by (repo id, position), giving each step a dense global seq. Paths are namespaced by repo. Scopes limit which paths produce nodes and links, while package keys are visible workspace-wide. Package keys (#name) resolve across repos, so a bare import in repo A opens a link to repo B's package when B's package.json with that name appears, and closes it when it goes.

Done when

on a folder of repos the Rust result equals bench.mjs's for the same input (the research: 43 repos, 14,730 commits, 153 cross-repo links dated), and every interval's from_seq commit belongs to the repo whose change caused it.

Security

a package name is attacker-chosen text in a repo; two repos declaring the same name is a real case (forks, vendored copies). The first by (repo id, path) wins deterministically and a duplicate-package note is recorded, so a hostile repo in the workspace can't silently capture another's links.

Scale

43 repos and 14,730 commits in 8.0 s in the research; 17,207 commits across 4 repos in the spec's example.

Efficiency

per-repo walks in parallel feeding the merge; extraction is shared, because blob ids dedupe across repos (forks and vendored copies parse once).

Size

M.

Tests

differential test against bench.mjs --json output on a fixture folder; a property test that the merge order is a total order consistent with each repo's own order; a fixture with clock skew between repos.

IDX-13

Single writer: lock, batching, WAL, progress, cancel M

Depends on IDX-03 IDX-11

WriterLock: fd-lock exclusive lock on <data>/<id>/index.lock, non-blocking.

Done when: killing the process with SIGKILL at 100 random points during a sync never leaves an index that fails quick_check, and the next sync finishes with the same fact hash as an uninterrupted run.

Merge note: Bumps meta.generation in every committed batch (SRV-02 depends on it). Kill loop: 100 iterations per pull request here, 1,000 nightly in HD-08.

Security
the lock, progress and cancel files live in the user-only data folder, so another local user can't cancel or block syncs.
Scale
a 412 MB index; a 40-minute first index (the spec's example) must be cancellable within 2 s.
Efficiency
synchronous = NORMAL in WAL gives one fsync per checkpoint, not per commit.
Full text of IDX-13

Depends on (as written)

IDX-03, IDX-11.

What to build

  • WriterLock: fd-lock exclusive lock on <data>/<id>/index.lock, non-blocking. The file holds the pid and start time for display only; correctness rests on the kernel lock (B2).
  • Batches: one transaction per 1,000 timeline steps or 2 s, whichever comes first (proposed; B2 says "per repo or per N commits"). Each batch updates repo.indexed_commit, repo.indexed_seq and meta.watermark_seq in the same transaction as its intervals, so a crash leaves a complete, earlier index.
  • End of run: wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE), PRAGMA optimize, state set to current.
  • Progress: <data>/<id>/progress.json, rewritten atomically at most four times a second (§4.8), because other processes can't see uncommitted writer state. It feeds Freshness.progress, MCP progress notifications and boards G02 and S03.
  • Cancel (E5): SIGINT, SIGTERM or a cancel file. The writer finishes the current batch, commits, checkpoints, and exits 1 with state behind (not failed). Parsed blobs are already in the cache.
  • A crash leaves meta.state = syncing with no lock holder; the next opener that takes the lock sets failed with the last error, then resumes.

Done when

killing the process with SIGKILL at 100 random points during a sync never leaves an index that fails quick_check, and the next sync finishes with the same fact hash as an uninterrupted run. A reader started before a sync sees the same row counts for its whole transaction while the sync commits.

Security

the lock, progress and cancel files live in the user-only data folder, so another local user can't cancel or block syncs. Progress holds counts and repo names only, no paths or code. A long-lived reader can block WAL truncation; the checkpoint falls back to PASSIVE and logs, rather than waiting forever (resource exhaustion through WAL growth is bounded by journal_size_limit = 64 MB, proposed).

Scale

a 412 MB index; a 40-minute first index (the spec's example) must be cancellable within 2 s.

Efficiency

synchronous = NORMAL in WAL gives one fsync per checkpoint, not per commit. wal_autocheckpoint stays at 1,000 pages during a run. Inserts are sorted by key inside a batch.

Size

M.

Tests

fault injection (a failpoints build that aborts at named points); a kill-loop test; a two-process test for snapshot reads; a test that a stale lock file with a dead pid doesn't block.

IDX-14

Incremental sync M

Depends on IDX-12 IDX-13

cartograph-index::sync(workspace, opts) -> SyncOutcome (§4.1).

Done when: after 100 new commits the incremental result has the same fact hash as a full rebuild, and on remotion takes under 500 ms warm (proposed; the research measured 105 ms in memory with a regular expression and no disk writes).

Security
--fetch shells out to git, which honours repo config (core.sshCommand, credential helpers).
Scale
rehydration at 60k files and 200k links must stay under 1 s (proposed), or the 100-commit target is lost to start-up.
Efficiency
rehydration reads three indexed scans; the facts of head blobs are read in one ordered pass.
Full text of IDX-14

Depends on (as written)

IDX-12, IDX-13.

What to build

cartograph-index::sync(workspace, opts) -> SyncOutcome (§4.1).

  • Rehydrate the replay state from the index: the tree from file_version rows with to_seq IS NULL, refs from the fact rows of those blobs, counts from open intervals. No history is re-walked.
  • For each repo, walk indexed_commit..branch tip and feed the merged timeline from watermark_seq + 1.
  • Out-of-order arrivals: if a new commit's corrected date is below the watermark date (a repo fetched late, or a new repo added to the workspace), run a partial replay from that date using the IDX-19 machinery. Proposed; the decisions don't cover it (R7).
  • Workspace changes: a folder added means a new repo walked from its root and merged by the same rule; a folder removed closes that repo's open intervals at a synthetic "removed from workspace" step and marks repo.removed.
  • --fetch runs git fetch through the git command for registry repos only, then syncs. Fetch is the only network action and only on this flag.
  • After the run: recompute the fact hash (IDX-17), mark derived rows (IDX-21), update layout (IDX-25), write the run row, and hand the status data to whoever writes CARTOGRAPH.md.

Done when

after 100 new commits the incremental result has the same fact hash as a full rebuild, and on remotion takes under 500 ms warm (proposed; the research measured 105 ms in memory with a regular expression and no disk writes). Adding and removing a folder in the workspace file is picked up by the next sync.

Security

--fetch shells out to git, which honours repo config (core.sshCommand, credential helpers). Run it with GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 and -c protocol.file.allow=user, never from the MCP server without the flag, and only for repos in the registry. Sync never fetches by itself.

Scale

rehydration at 60k files and 200k links must stay under 1 s (proposed), or the 100-commit target is lost to start-up.

Efficiency

rehydration reads three indexed scans; the facts of head blobs are read in one ordered pass. If rehydration dominates, add a replay_state snapshot blob written at the end of each run and validated by the watermark.

Size

M.

Tests

the research's incremental check (last 100 commits end in the same state); a property test that any split of a history into sync runs gives the same fact hash; end-to-end tests for folder add and remove.

IDX-15

Freshness check, dirty flag, background sync M

Depends on IDX-02 IDX-11 IDX-13 IDX-14

freshness(workspace) -> FreshnessReport (§4.1): for each repo, read the indexed branch's tip and HEAD from the ref store (loose ref or packed-refs) and compare with repo.indexed_commit.

Done when: the check takes under 100 ms at p95 for 50 repos on a warm file cache (the D1 benchmark), with no network syscalls.

Merge note: Owns the freshness computation and request_sync. SRV-03 and SRV-13 call it and don't re-implement the gix read. Behind is capped at 1,000 and the memo is 1 s (SRV-03 proposed 10,000 and 2 s).

Security
a background child inherits no secrets it doesn't need: environment trimmed to PATH, HOME and the XDG variables, working directory set to the data folder.
Scale
43–50 repos per workspace; MCP agents may issue tens of reads per second.
Efficiency
ref reads only; packed-refs is memory-mapped and binary-searched by gix.
Full text of IDX-15

Depends on (as written)

IDX-11, IDX-13, IDX-14; IDX-02 (D1 result).

What to build

  • freshness(workspace) -> FreshnessReport (§4.1): for each repo, read the indexed branch's tip and HEAD from the ref store (loose ref or packed-refs) and compare with repo.indexed_commit. No object reads when they are equal.
  • behind: counted by a first-parent walk from the tip to the indexed commit, capped at 1,000 steps or 20 ms per repo; over the cap it reports 1000+ (proposed). If the indexed commit isn't reachable within the cap and an ancestry check fails, the repo's state is rewritten.
  • dirty: outside the 100 ms budget. Proposed: gix status with a 50 ms budget per repo, run after the answer and cached keyed by the mtime of .git/index; the first read may report dirty: null. The decisions put only the HEAD comparison in the 100 ms.
  • on_branch_other_than_indexed for status (spec §8).
  • Results cached in-process for 1 s to cover bursts of MCP calls.
  • request_sync(workspace): try the writer lock. If held, create <data>/<id>/dirty and return AlreadyRunning (exit 0 for the CLI). If free and auto_sync = "background", spawn cartograph sync --quiet --background detached with stdio on null. The running sync checks for dirty before releasing the lock, deletes it, and runs one more round (D2); at most 5 extra rounds per process (proposed) so a commit loop can't pin it forever, and it leaves the flag in place when it stops at the cap.
  • Combined state computation for §7 of the spec: none, current, behind, syncing, rewritten, needs-migrate, failed.

Done when

the check takes under 100 ms at p95 for 50 repos on a warm file cache (the D1 benchmark), with no network syscalls. Ten concurrent sync invocations produce exactly one writer, nine exit 0 within 50 ms, and the index ends at HEAD even when commits land during the run.

Security

a background child inherits no secrets it doesn't need: environment trimmed to PATH, HOME and the XDG variables, working directory set to the data folder. Spawn rate is limited to one attempt per workspace per 2 s so a tight loop of reads can't fork-bomb. The binary path is current_exe(), never looked up on PATH.

Scale

43–50 repos per workspace; MCP agents may issue tens of reads per second.

Efficiency

ref reads only; packed-refs is memory-mapped and binary-searched by gix. Repos are checked in parallel on a small pool. No SQLite write on the read path.

Size

M.

Tests

a benchmark gate in CI (IDX-28); a stress test of 50 concurrent requesters; unit tests for each state; a test that auto_sync = "never" spawns nothing.

SRV-03

Freshness and cartograph status S

Depends on SRV-01 SRV-02 IDX-15

freshness(ws) -> Freshness. For each repo: indexed commit, HEAD, commits behind, dirty, shallow, rewritten, on another branch.

Done when: the check takes under 100 ms for 30 repos (the D1 benchmark). status --check exits 3 on a repo that is one commit behind.

Merge note: Owns cartograph status and the wire shape. The check itself is IDX-15; the D1 benchmark is IDX-02.

Security
--line output is loaded into every Claude Code session, so it holds counts and fixed words only.
Scale
30 repos in under 100 ms cold; under 5 ms from the memo.
Efficiency
if the benchmark fails, cache by the mtime of HEAD and the ref file, as D1 already plans.
Full text of SRV-03

Depends on (as written)

SRV-01, SRV-02, IDX (registry, per-repo indexed commit, lock file, dirty flag).

What to build

  • freshness(ws) -> Freshness. For each repo: indexed commit, HEAD, commits behind, dirty, shallow, rewritten, on another branch. Plus the state from spec §7 and whether the lock is held.
  • The HEAD check reads .git/HEAD and the ref, or packed-refs, through gix. No network, no git subprocess.
  • "Behind" is counted along first-parent from the indexed commit, capped at 10,000 and shown as "10,000+".
  • A 2-second in-process memo, so a burst of API calls costs one check.
  • cartograph status with --json, --check (exit 3 when behind) and --line (one line, for the SessionStart hook).

Done when

the check takes under 100 ms for 30 repos (the D1 benchmark). status --check exits 3 on a repo that is one commit behind. status --line prints one line under 120 characters and exits 0 even with no workspace.

Security

--line output is loaded into every Claude Code session, so it holds counts and fixed words only. No repo or branch names (F8).

Scale

30 repos in under 100 ms cold; under 5 ms from the memo.

Efficiency

if the benchmark fails, cache by the mtime of HEAD and the ref file, as D1 already plans.

Size

S.

Tests

unit, golden (human and JSON output), end-to-end over fixture repos: detached HEAD, shallow clone, force-push, a worktree.

IDX-16

Rebuild by atomic rename; migrations M

Depends on IDX-13 IDX-14

rebuild(workspace): build index.cartograph.new under the writer lock, fsync the file and the directory, rename over index.cartograph, then remove stray -wal and -shm files of the old name.

Done when: a reader holding the old file open during a rebuild keeps answering and sees the new index on its next request; killing a rebuild leaves the old index untouched and a .new file that the next run deletes; a v1 fixture migrates…

Security
the temp file is created with O_EXCL and 0600 in the same directory (same file system, so the rename is atomic).
Scale
412 MB: a rebuild needs roughly twice that on disk for a time.
Efficiency
a rebuild reuses the fact cache, so it is walk plus replay plus write.
Full text of IDX-16

Depends on (as written)

IDX-13, IDX-14.

What to build

  • rebuild(workspace): build index.cartograph.new under the writer lock, fsync the file and the directory, rename over index.cartograph, then remove stray -wal and -shm files of the old name. The old index stays readable until the rename. On Windows, where an open file can't be replaced, write index.<n>.cartograph and switch a small current pointer file; readers reopen when the pointer changes (proposed; B2 notes the Windows problem and gives no mechanism).
  • Carry-over on rebuild: the run table, meta.workspace_id, and layout positions keyed by node key, so the map doesn't jump (I2).
  • Migrations with rusqlite_migration on user_version, each run on a copy that is then renamed in, never in place. No migration registered for a version pair means rebuild, since the index is a cache (B3).
  • Readers detect replacement: the query layer compares the file's inode (or the pointer file) and meta.generation before each request batch and reopens.
  • clean --cache and clean --all back ends.

Done when

a reader holding the old file open during a rebuild keeps answering and sees the new index on its next request; killing a rebuild leaves the old index untouched and a .new file that the next run deletes; a v1 fixture migrates to v2 in a test migration and yields the same fact hash as a fresh v2 build.

Security

the temp file is created with O_EXCL and 0600 in the same directory (same file system, so the rename is atomic). A pre-existing index.cartograph.new is removed only if it is a regular file owned by the user (no symlink following). Free disk space is checked before a rebuild (needs the current size plus 20%).

Scale

412 MB: a rebuild needs roughly twice that on disk for a time. The spec's example rebuild is about 40 minutes; with a warm fact cache it should be minutes.

Efficiency

a rebuild reuses the fact cache, so it is walk plus replay plus write. The new file is built with journal_mode = OFF and synchronous = OFF, then switched to WAL and fsynced before the rename, since a crash just discards it.

Size

M.

Tests

crash injection around the rename; a reader-during-rebuild test; migration fixtures; a Windows CI job for the pointer path.

IDX-17

Fact hash and cartograph verify S

Depends on IDX-07 IDX-10

fact_hash(index) -> [u8; 32] and verify(a, b | manifest).

Done when: two indexes built on different machines, with different insert orders and SQLite page sizes, from the same manifest and commits have the same hash; changing one interval changes it; a full sync and any chain of incremental syncs…

Security
the hash proves equal facts, not provenance; the docs must not call it a signature.
Scale
remotion: 103,347 fact rows and 36,193 intervals. At 200k links and a 412 MB index the full recompute must stay under 2 s (proposed).
Efficiency
streamed through covering indexes in sorted order into one hasher; no in-memory sort.
Full text of IDX-17

Depends on (as written)

IDX-07, IDX-10.

What to build

fact_hash(index) -> [u8; 32] and verify(a, b | manifest).

  • Canonical input, in order (§4.9): a header (cartograph-fact-hash v1), the sorted extractor names and versions, the config_hash, then per repo sorted by root commit: the indexed commit, and sorted records for file lineages (paths and their sha ranges), facts (blob oid, extractor, encoded payload) and intervals (kind, source key, target key, opening sha, closing sha or empty, origin). No row ids, no seq numbers, no timestamps, no local paths.
  • Stored in meta.fact_hash as sha256:<hex> after every sync; also in cartograph.yaml only when the user runs verify --manifest --write (proposed: the decisions say verify compares "an index against a manifest" and don't say where the manifest keeps a hash).
  • verify prints equal or not, and on a mismatch the first differing section (extractors, config, repo, commit) so the cause is actionable.

Done when

two indexes built on different machines, with different insert orders and SQLite page sizes, from the same manifest and commits have the same hash; changing one interval changes it; a full sync and any chain of incremental syncs to the same commits agree.

Security

the hash proves equal facts, not provenance; the docs must not call it a signature. Comparison of an untrusted index goes through IDX-03's open rules first.

Scale

remotion: 103,347 fact rows and 36,193 intervals. At 200k links and a 412 MB index the full recompute must stay under 2 s (proposed). If it doesn't, switch to per-repo digests combined incrementally and bump the hash header to v2 (R8).

Efficiency

streamed through covering indexes in sorted order into one hasher; no in-memory sort.

Size

S to M.

Tests

property test for insert-order independence; golden hash for the fixture repo; mutation tests (flip one row, hash must change).

SRV-13

Events, jobs and background sync M

Depends on SRV-03 SRV-05 IDX-14 IDX-15

A job registry: table job(id, kind, state, progress, step, message, pid, started_at, ended_at) in the activity tables beside ask.db.

Done when: a commit made in a fixture repo while the app is open leads to a generation event with no user action, and the map request that follows returns the new data.

Merge note: ensure_fresh is a call to IDX-15's request_sync. The stream is global, not per workspace, because one tab owns the connection for all tabs (FE-05).

Security
Job kinds are a fixed enum. No request can name a command or a path outside the registry.
Scale
a first index runs for about 40 minutes (spec example). Progress events are throttled to 2 a second.
Efficiency
no polling when no stream is open.
Full text of SRV-13

Depends on (as written)

SRV-03, SRV-05, IDX (sync entry point, lock, dirty flag D2, cancel E5).

What to build

  • A job registry: table job(id, kind, state, progress, step, message, pid, started_at, ended_at) in the activity tables beside ask.db. It is visible across processes, so MCP status can report a sync the app started.
  • ensure_fresh(ws): called by every read path. If a repo is behind, auto_sync is background and no sync is running, it starts one (D1). Inside the server the indexer runs in-process; from a one-shot CLI command it starts a detached child with stdio on null.
  • GET /events (SSE): freshness, job.progress, job.done, job.failed, generation, activity, and a comment every 15 s. The current state is sent on connect, so no replay log is needed.
  • POST /jobs (sync, rebuild), DELETE /jobs/{id} (cancel), GET /jobs/{id}.
  • Cross-process change detection: poll PRAGMA data_version once a second while any stream is open.

Done when

a commit made in a fixture repo while the app is open leads to a generation event with no user action, and the map request that follows returns the new data. Cancel leaves the previous index readable (E5). Two sync requests at once produce one run and one extra round (D2).

Security

  • Job kinds are a fixed enum. No request can name a command or a path outside the registry.
  • rebuild is not exposed to MCP.
  • At most 16 streams; a stream is dropped if its client stops reading for 30 s.

Scale

a first index runs for about 40 minutes (spec example). Progress events are throttled to 2 a second.

Efficiency

no polling when no stream is open.

Size

M.

Tests

end-to-end with a fixture repo, crash injection (kill the writer mid-run, then check the state is failed and reads still work), a property test on the dirty-flag protocol.

FE-05

Live events over SSE M

Depends on FE-03 SRV-13

lib/api/events.ts. A fetch-based stream reader (not EventSource, so it can send headers and abort), with Last-Event-ID resume and backoff of 0.5 s to 15 s with jitter.

Done when: a sync started from the CLI moves the header chip to "syncing" with progress within 1 s and back to "current" at the end, with no reload; killing the binary shows "Cartograph isn't running" within 5 s and recovers when it…

Merge note: Reads one stream, GET /api/v1/events, with the bearer header. Event names are the front-end plan's set (see Interfaces); heartbeat every 15 s; progress at most 4 a second.

Security
the stream is same-origin and cookie-authenticated. Event payloads carry ids and counts; commit messages and author names in them are untrusted text.
Scale
browsers allow 6 HTTP/1.1 connections per origin, and HTTP/2 isn't available on plain loopback.
Efficiency
heartbeat comment every 20 s; events are coalesced per animation frame before React sees them.
Full text of FE-05

Depends on (as written)

FE-03, SRV-13.

What to build

lib/api/events.ts. A fetch-based stream reader (not EventSource, so it can send headers and abort), with Last-Event-ID resume and backoff of 0.5 s to 15 s with jitter. Typed events as in section 4.3. One stream per browser, not per tab: the tab holding a Web Lock named cartograph-events owns the connection and rebroadcasts on a BroadcastChannel. On index.changed, the cache moves to the new generation and visible resources refetch.

Done when

a sync started from the CLI moves the header chip to "syncing" with progress within 1 s and back to "current" at the end, with no reload; killing the binary shows "Cartograph isn't running" within 5 s and recovers when it restarts; 8 open tabs hold 1 connection.

Security

the stream is same-origin and cookie-authenticated. Event payloads carry ids and counts; commit messages and author names in them are untrusted text.

Scale

browsers allow 6 HTTP/1.1 connections per origin, and HTTP/2 isn't available on plain loopback. Without the single-owner rule, 6 tabs would block every fetch. Progress events are throttled by the server to 4 per second.

Efficiency

heartbeat comment every 20 s; events are coalesced per animation frame before React sees them.

Size

M

Tests

unit tests on the parser with split chunks and reconnects; property test that any chunking of a byte stream yields the same events; Playwright with a server that drops the stream mid-event.

SRV-14

Writes from the app L

Depends on SRV-13 IDX-08 IDX-10

POST /corrections and DELETE /corrections/{id}: both go through the same function as MCP correct.

Done when: a correction saved in the app appears in cartograph.overrides.yaml and in git diff, and the link changes after the re-index.

Merge note: Config writes use one resource (GET, POST …/preview, PUT with If-Match), as FE-13 asks. Merges and domains go to cartograph.yaml, not the override file. Folder picking: a typed path with fs/list completion, names only. Preferences move here because of the port ruling. Corrections routes land with XS-13.

Security
Every write needs the token, a matching Origin and Content-Type: application/json.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of SRV-14

Depends on (as written)

SRV-13, XS (override file format, H3), IDX (manifest writer, C7).

What to build

  • POST /corrections and DELETE /corrections/{id}: both go through the same function as MCP correct. They return the YAML diff.
  • POST /settings/services: with preview: true it returns a diff only; otherwise it writes cartograph.yaml and re-indexes that repo (C7).
  • PUT /settings/indexing: auto_sync, hooks, the hourly fetch, excludes, extractor switches. Hooks and schedule changes call IDX.
  • GET and PUT /api/v1/prefs: keymap, appearance, pins, layout of the Ask panel. Stored in ~/.cartograph/prefs.json (see R2).
  • POST /api/v1/discover and POST /api/v1/workspaces: the wizard (I9). GET /api/v1/fs/list?path= lists folders so the user can pick one, because a browser file picker doesn't give a path.

Done when

a correction saved in the app appears in cartograph.overrides.yaml and in git diff, and the link changes after the re-index. Every write is rejected without the token or with a foreign Origin.

Security

  • Every write needs the token, a matching Origin and Content-Type: application/json.
  • Files are written with temp-file-and-rename, only at the three known names in the workspace folder. Symlinks at those names are refused.
  • fs/list returns folder names and three flags (is a repo, has a workspace file, readable). It never returns file contents, never follows symlinks, canonicalises the path, and refuses anything outside the home folder and mounted volumes the user names.
  • reason and other free text are stored as data, length-capped, and YAML-escaped by a serialiser, never by string concatenation.

Scale

n/a.

Efficiency

n/a.

Size

L.

Tests

end-to-end per write, a fuzz target on YAML round-trips, traversal tests on fs/list (.., symlinks, /proc, UNC paths).

HD-06

Property tests L

Depends on HD-03 IDX-06

properties, each with proptest or fast-check

Done when: all properties run in CI with 256 cases per pull request and 10,000 nightly, with failing seeds saved to the repo.

Security
these are the correctness backstop for "wrong answer" risks, which G6 names as the worst case of injection.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-06

Depends on (as written)

HD-03, the code under test.

What to build

properties, each with proptest or fast-check:

  • Replay equals direct indexing: for a random commit in a random generated history, facts from intervals equal facts from indexing that commit's tree. This is the research's own check (24 of 24 sampled commits).
  • Incremental equals full: indexing commits 1 to n in any split gives the same fact hash as one run.
  • Rewrite: replay from the merge base equals a clean index of the new history (B7).
  • Interval algebra: no overlap per fact, closed before opened never happens, overlay rows win over base (B5).
  • Confidence: the combined score stays inside its level's band whatever evidence is added (H2).
  • Corrections: a correction bound to a lineage id still applies after any sequence of renames (H3).
  • Level of detail: every bundle's count equals the number of member links; lifting is idempotent.
  • JSONC: our reading of a .code-workspace equals VS Code's rules on generated inputs (C1).
  • Front end: hash state round trip; SSE parser under any chunking; cache never serves generation n data under n+1.
  • Citations: a sentence is kept only if every handle is from this turn and covers the date (G5).

Done when

all properties run in CI with 256 cases per pull request and 10,000 nightly, with failing seeds saved to the repo.

Security

these are the correctness backstop for "wrong answer" risks, which G6 names as the worst case of injection.

Size

L

Tests

itself.

Method: shared CI runners are noisy, so counts and instruction-level benches (iai-callgrind) gate pull requests at ±3%, and wall-clock gates compare base and head in the same job, interleaved, failing above 10%. Phase 3 · The whole app on real history, and MCP for agents

28 items · 4S 14M 10L · roadmap stages 2 and 3; MCP from stage 6
Goal

Finish the indexer's breadth (renames, rewrites, services and modules, layout, coverage, Go, Python, PHP), serve the full read API, move every map and time screen off demo data, and ship the MCP tools, which need only the query layer and no model.

What a user can do at the end

Open a workspace of several repos at service level, zoom to modules and files through WebGL, scrub ten years of history, compare two dates, search, and do all of it from the table with the keyboard. In Claude Code or Cursor, find, describe, neighbors, path and impact answer with a freshness line.

Exit gate
  • The 62-service, 8,312-file fixture opens at service level under 1 s after data arrives; the 60k and 200k fixture meets FE-07's marks; opening an index twice gives identical positions.
  • File-level slice of 60k nodes and 200k links under 500 ms cold and 50 ms cached; search p95 under 30 ms; density for 10 years of weekly bins under 30 ms.
  • A force-push that rewrites 50 commits ends in the same fact hash as a rebuild; a moved file keeps one lineage.
  • No MCP result over 10k tokens at limit: 50; instructions under 4,096 characters; the injection fixture's text appears only in untrusted_text.
  • Every route has a visual baseline in both themes; the journeys pass on Chromium, Firefox and WebKit with outbound network blocked.
  • lib/demo/ is gone except for cross-service, symbol and Ask data.
Risks retired
  • sigma 4 custom programs for the board's glyphs and line styles.
  • Layout stability, which compare and scrubbing depend on.
  • The 28-file move off synchronous global arrays.
  • Six connections per origin on loopback (one shared event stream).

AI-15 indexes names, paths and split identifiers here. Docstrings join the search table once the secret scanner (XS-10) lands in phase 4. SRV-11's ref and working-tree inputs wait for IDX-24 in phase 5; MCP correct is stubbed until XS-13.

IDX-18

Rename detection and file lineage L

Depends on IDX-02 IDX-06 IDX-12

in the per-commit diff, pair deletions with additions: first by equal blob id (the research's exact-rename pass), then with gix-diff rewrite tracking at 50% similarity and at most 1,000 candidates.

Done when: on a fixture, a moved file has one file row with two file_path rows, its inbound links stay open across the move when importers were updated in the same commit, and board G10's history ("Moved from … 98% same content") can be…

Security
similarity matching reads blob contents and is quadratic in candidates; the 1,000 limit and the 1 MB blob cap bound it.
Scale
remotion's 9,498 commits; monorepo splits that move thousands of files in one commit.
Efficiency
the exact pass is a hash join on blob ids already in hand and costs almost nothing (B7).
Full text of IDX-18

Depends on (as written)

IDX-06, IDX-12; IDX-02 (B7 result).

What to build

in the per-commit diff, pair deletions with additions: first by equal blob id (the research's exact-rename pass), then with gix-diff rewrite tracking at 50% similarity and at most 1,000 candidates. When the limit is hit, write a note row of kind rename-limit on the commit (B7). A pair keeps the file's lineage id: file_path gets a new row (via rename-exact or rename-similar, with the similarity), and links survive, because links join nodes by lineage, not path. The replay treats a rename as a path-key change, so importers that now fail to resolve close their links correctly while the node keeps its identity. Directory (module) nodes follow when every child moved (proposed).

Done when

on a fixture, a moved file has one file row with two file_path rows, its inbound links stay open across the move when importers were updated in the same commit, and board G10's history ("Moved from … 98% same content") can be produced from file_path. Agreement with git log -M50% --first-parent on remotion is at or above the level recorded in IDX-02.

Security

similarity matching reads blob contents and is quadratic in candidates; the 1,000 limit and the 1 MB blob cap bound it. A commit that deletes and adds 100,000 files (a vendored drop) hits the limit and is noted rather than stalling.

Scale

remotion's 9,498 commits; monorepo splits that move thousands of files in one commit.

Efficiency

the exact pass is a hash join on blob ids already in hand and costs almost nothing (B7). Similarity runs only on leftovers and only among tracked file types.

Size

L.

Tests

golden lineage for a fixture with exact moves, edited moves, a swap of two files, and a move plus a new file at the old path; differential test against git log -M; a property test that lineage ids never merge two files alive at the same time.

IDX-19

Rewritten history: replay from the merge base M

Depends on IDX-14 IDX-18

in sync, if repo.indexed_commit is not an ancestor of the branch tip: find the merge base on the first-parent chains; record a rewrite row (old head, new head, base, time); copy the intervals that opened after the base into…

Done when: after a force-push that drops and rewrites the last 50 commits of one repo in a three-repo fixture, the next sync yields the same fact hash as a rebuild; the rewrite row and archived links are present; G04's dashed marker can…

Security
a rewrite replays more work than a normal sync; a repo force-pushed in a loop could keep the writer busy.
Scale
worst case is a rewrite at the root of a 9.5k-commit repo, which costs one replay (seconds in the research) and no parsing for unchanged blobs.
Efficiency
truncation is three indexed range deletes or updates on seq; no file is copied.
Full text of IDX-19

Depends on (as written)

IDX-14, IDX-18.

What to build

in sync, if repo.indexed_commit is not an ancestor of the branch tip: find the merge base on the first-parent chains; record a rewrite row (old head, new head, base, time); copy the intervals that opened after the base into archived_interval keyed by sha, for board G10's "links that existed only in the old history are kept separately"; then truncate the global timeline at the base step's seq: delete later commits, intervals opened later, and reopen intervals closed later; rehydrate; replay all repos from that point (other repos' later steps are replayed too, since cross-repo links depend on order). If no merge base exists, re-walk that repo from its root. State is rewritten until this completes.

Done when

after a force-push that drops and rewrites the last 50 commits of one repo in a three-repo fixture, the next sync yields the same fact hash as a rebuild; the rewrite row and archived links are present; G04's dashed marker can be drawn from rewrite.at.

Security

a rewrite replays more work than a normal sync; a repo force-pushed in a loop could keep the writer busy. The dirty-round cap (IDX-15) and the fact cache bound the cost. Archived intervals are capped per rewrite (10,000, proposed) with a count of the rest.

Scale

worst case is a rewrite at the root of a 9.5k-commit repo, which costs one replay (seconds in the research) and no parsing for unchanged blobs.

Efficiency

truncation is three indexed range deletes or updates on seq; no file is copied.

Size

M.

Tests

fixtures for amend, rebase, a force-push to unrelated history, and a rewrite in one repo while another repo has later commits; property test: rewrite then sync equals rebuild.

IDX-21

Extractor versioning and inputs_key invalidation M

Depends on IDX-05 IDX-14

The extractor table and meta record each extractor's name and version (B3).

Done when: bumping ts-imports from 1.0.0 to 1.0.1 re-parses only TS/JS blobs and leaves Go facts and their cache rows untouched; an unchanged run rebuilds zero derived rows; changing a model id marks summaries stale without deleting them.

Merge note: Also owns the per-generation fan_in column and count tables that SRV-08, SRV-09 and SRV-12 read.

Security
inputs_key covers the config hash, so a manifest change that alters excludes can't leave derived data describing excluded code.
Scale
103k cached blobs; a parser upgrade on remotion should cost one extraction pass and one replay, not a walk.
Efficiency
keys are computed from ids already in memory at the end of a run; rebuilds are batched per artefact kind.
Full text of IDX-21

Depends on (as written)

IDX-05, IDX-14.

What to build

  • The extractor table and meta record each extractor's name and version (B3). A version bump re-extracts only blobs whose file type that extractor handles; the cache key already includes the version.
  • Changed facts for a blob that is alive anywhere in history force a replay from the earliest seq where it was alive (usually a full replay, with no re-parse of other languages). This is the IDX-19 truncate-and-replay path with base 0.
  • inputs_key = SHA-256(kind ‖ sorted input blob ids or upstream fact hashes ‖ extractor name and version ‖ config_hash ‖ model id and prompt version for AI rows) (B6). The derived registry table (§4.3) holds one row per derived artefact; derived tables carry the key too.
  • invalidate(run): recompute keys for artefacts whose inputs the run touched; rebuild the ones whose key changed; mark AI output stale = 1 instead of deleting.
  • Derived artefacts owned here: layout (IDX-25), node and link counts per service, fan-in ranks used by find. Owned elsewhere but using this mechanism: FTS5, summaries, reachability, concepts.

Done when

bumping ts-imports from 1.0.0 to 1.0.1 re-parses only TS/JS blobs and leaves Go facts and their cache rows untouched; an unchanged run rebuilds zero derived rows; changing a model id marks summaries stale without deleting them.

Security

inputs_key covers the config hash, so a manifest change that alters excludes can't leave derived data describing excluded code. Stale AI rows must be served as stale (their text is untrusted either way, F8).

Scale

103k cached blobs; a parser upgrade on remotion should cost one extraction pass and one replay, not a walk.

Efficiency

keys are computed from ids already in memory at the end of a run; rebuilds are batched per artefact kind.

Size

M.

Tests

unit tests of key composition; an integration test per rule in "Done when"; a property test that reordering inputs doesn't change a key.

IDX-22

Coverage accounting S

Depends on IDX-06 IDX-20

unresolved rows for references that didn't resolve (raw specifier, file lineage, line, stage, reason), stored as intervals so coverage is known as of any date; skipped reasons per blob from IDX-05; per-run totals in run…

Done when: after a run on remotion the CLI can print "files parsed x / y" and "imports resolved z%", and every unresolved import in the count can be listed with file and line (board A01).

Merge note: Owns unresolved rows for files and imports and the run totals. XS-14 owns the six-row coverage query and the cross-service rows.

Security
raw specifiers are untrusted text and are returned only in untrusted_text-style fields by the layers above; truncated to 512 bytes at write.
Scale
unresolved references could dominate in a repo with heavy path aliases that fail; capped at 1 million rows with a counter for the rest (proposed).
Efficiency
written from the replay's existing event stream; no extra pass.
Full text of IDX-22

Depends on (as written)

IDX-06, IDX-20.

What to build

unresolved rows for references that didn't resolve (raw specifier, file lineage, line, stage, reason), stored as intervals so coverage is known as of any date; skipped reasons per blob from IDX-05; per-run totals in run (files total, files parsed, imports total, imports resolved). The query layer turns these into CoverageRows.

Done when

after a run on remotion the CLI can print "files parsed x / y" and "imports resolved z%", and every unresolved import in the count can be listed with file and line (board A01).

Security

raw specifiers are untrusted text and are returned only in untrusted_text-style fields by the layers above; truncated to 512 bytes at write.

Scale

unresolved references could dominate in a repo with heavy path aliases that fail; capped at 1 million rows with a counter for the rest (proposed).

Efficiency

written from the replay's existing event stream; no extra pass.

Size

S.

Tests

golden unresolved list for a fixture; a test that fixing an alias in tsconfig.json closes the unresolved intervals at that commit.

IDX-25

Layout at index time L

Depends on IDX-20 IDX-21

cartograph-index::layout (I2)

Done when: opening the same index twice gives identical positions (stage 3 gate "the layout is the same the next time"); adding one file moves no service and no module outside its own; a 60k-file, 200k-link fixture lays out in under 30 s on…

Security
layout is CPU-heavy and runs on repo-shaped input: iteration counts are fixed, and a parent with more than 20,000 children falls back to a grid packing so a pathological folder can't…
Scale
62 services, about 1,140 modules, 60k files, 200k links (I1's fixture).
Efficiency
each parent's children are laid out independently, in parallel with rayon; Barnes-Hut is O(n log n) per iteration; file-level layout for a service is computed lazily on a later sync…
Full text of IDX-25

Depends on (as written)

IDX-20, IDX-21.

What to build

cartograph-index::layout (I2):

  • Top-down: place services (grouped by domain when domains exist), then modules inside each service's area, then files inside each module's area. A change at file level can't move a service.
  • Algorithm: our own Barnes-Hut force layout of about 300 lines, or the graphology/rust-fa2 port, never the AGPL forceatlas2 crate. Fixed seed, fixed iteration count, deterministic float order, so two machines place nodes alike (kept out of the fact hash regardless).
  • Stability: existing nodes are pinned; a new node starts at its parent's position and only new nodes and their immediate neighbours move, within the parent's area. Positions are stored as intervals in layout (§4.3), so scrubbing time reads positions with the same as-of rule as links.
  • "Snapshot" is not defined in I2. Proposed: layout advances once per sync run, plus once per calendar month of history during a first index, rather than per commit.
  • Carried across rebuilds by node key (IDX-16).
  • inputs_key over the node set and weights per parent, so only changed parents are laid out again.

Done when

opening the same index twice gives identical positions (stage 3 gate "the layout is the same the next time"); adding one file moves no service and no module outside its own; a 60k-file, 200k-link fixture lays out in under 30 s on first index and under 1 s for a 100-commit sync (proposed).

Security

layout is CPU-heavy and runs on repo-shaped input: iteration counts are fixed, and a parent with more than 20,000 children falls back to a grid packing so a pathological folder can't stall indexing.

Scale

62 services, about 1,140 modules, 60k files, 200k links (I1's fixture).

Efficiency

each parent's children are laid out independently, in parallel with rayon; Barnes-Hut is O(n log n) per iteration; file-level layout for a service is computed lazily on a later sync if a first index would exceed its budget, with the browser's local ForceAtlas2 covering the gap (I2 allows local layout on expand).

Size

L.

Tests

determinism test (two runs, equal bytes); stability property (unchanged nodes never move); containment property (children inside the parent's area); a benchmark gate.

LanguageGrammarFactsResolver inputs (content keys)Resolution
Gotree-sitter-goimport (path, alias)go.mod (module, replace), go.work (use)Import path minus the module path gives a directory; the link goes to the package directory node (a module-level target), since Go imports name packages, not files
Pythontree-sitter-pythonimport, from … import, relative levelspyproject.toml (package roots), setup.cfg, __init__.py existenceDotted name to pkg/mod.py or pkg/mod/__init__.py, across configured roots and src/
PHPtree-sitter-phpuse, require/include with literalscomposer.json autoload.psr-4 and psr-0Namespace prefix to directory, then class to file

Cross-repo keys by module path (Go), distribution name (Python) and Composer package name (PHP), the same way as npm package keys.

Full text of AI-15

Depends on (as written)

IDX-21 FTS5 tables written at index time; SRV-09 fts_search.

What to build

the requirement on the index: an FTS5 table over node name, path, a split-identifier column (InvoiceRepo.insert also indexed as invoice repo insert), docstring and (when present) summary, with prefix indexes; a second table for contract operations. Ranking: bm25 with weights name > split name > path > docstring, then fan-in as the tie-break (F1 says find ranks by fan-in). Time slicing by joining to intervals. Query text from the user or the model is passed as a bound parameter and quoted into FTS syntax by our code, so FTS operators in a question cannot error or widen the search. S06's other sources (symbol bodies, diff hunks, READMEs, commit messages) stay off until someone asks; Retrieval.tsx defaults change to match (R6). find's demo summary "by name or meaning" becomes "by name or path".

Done when

on the 60k-file fixture, find "invoice repo" returns InvoiceRepo.ts in the top 3 in under 20 ms p95; a query of " or NEAR( returns results or an empty list, never a SQL error.

Security

FTS query injection (handled above). Docstrings are untrusted text: they are searchable but returned only in untrusted_text (F8). The secret scan (AI-19) runs before text enters the table.

Scale

about 60k files plus symbols (board S06 shows 61k symbol entries): low hundreds of thousands of short rows, tens of MB inside the 412 MB index.

Efficiency

external-content FTS, so text is not stored twice; updated only for changed blobs (B6).

Size

M (most of the build sits with IDX-21).

Tests

golden ranking set; fuzz the query quoting; a performance gate in CI.

Full text of SRV-09

Depends on (as written)

SRV-02, IDX (FTS5 tables over names, paths and docstrings, G7).

What to build

search(q, kinds, at, scope, limit).

  • The query is tokenised by us and each token is passed as a quoted FTS5 string. Raw user input never reaches MATCH.
  • Prefix match on the last token. A trigram table gives substring matches of three or more characters, which is what the palette does today with includes.
  • Rank: bm25, then fan-in (F1: "ranked by fan-in"), then shorter paths first.
  • Covers services, modules, files, symbols, concepts and operations, and entrypoints through kind.
  • A "near name" suggestion when nothing matches (board G05).
  • A request that the client drops interrupts its query.

Done when

the palette's results on northwind match today's for every existing query. p95 is under 30 ms on 60k files plus their symbols. An input of " OR NEAR( returns results or nothing, never an error.

Security

FTS5 query injection (above). q is capped at 256 characters and 16 tokens.

Scale

60k files, about 500k symbols (stage 5).

Efficiency

limit is pushed into SQL. Fan-in is read from a per-generation column, not counted per query.

Size

M.

Tests

golden, fuzz on q, a criterion benchmark.

SRV-11

Traversals: impact, path, reach L

Depends on SRV-04 SRV-08

An in-memory adjacency (CSR, both directions, each edge with its interval, kind and confidence), built lazily per generation and shared by HTTP, MCP and Ask.

Done when: impact on a node with 10k dependents takes under 20 ms warm. Results match the TypeScript impact and path on northwind.

Security
Resource caps: 50k visited nodes, depth 12, a 5 s deadline. Past a cap the result is marked truncated.
Scale
200k links is about 5 MB of adjacency. The build takes under 300 ms once per generation.
Efficiency
visited sets are bitsets. The adjacency is held in an Arc and swapped on a generation change.
Full text of SRV-11

Depends on (as written)

SRV-04, IDX (branch overlays B5, working-tree parse for impact).

What to build

  • An in-memory adjacency (CSR, both directions, each edge with its interval, kind and confidence), built lazily per generation and shared by HTTP, MCP and Ask.
  • impact(input), where the input is paths, a ref, the working tree or since. It returns affected nodes grouped as exact, likely and guessed, and by service. The confidence of a path is its weakest link.
  • path(from, to): bidirectional BFS, up to 3 paths, 12 hops at most.
  • reach(from), stories and reading_order for Ask and "Trace on the map".
  • cartograph impact <ref> with --working-tree, --wait and --format md|json.

Done when

impact on a node with 10k dependents takes under 20 ms warm. Results match the TypeScript impact and path on northwind. The Markdown output is stable (golden) and holds no repo prose.

Security

  • Resource caps: 50k visited nodes, depth 12, a 5 s deadline. Past a cap the result is marked truncated.
  • The working tree is parsed in memory and never written to the index (spec §5).
  • ref is resolved by gix and never passed to a shell.

Scale

200k links is about 5 MB of adjacency. The build takes under 300 ms once per generation.

Efficiency

visited sets are bitsets. The adjacency is held in an Arc and swapped on a generation change.

Size

L.

Tests

property (BFS against a naive reference), golden Markdown, end-to-end on a fixture branch.

Full text of FE-16

Depends on (as written)

FE-05, SRV-13, an activity store (I5 puts it in tables beside ask.db).

What to build

the bell reads GET /api/activity and live activity.new events. Rows are built from index events and sync commits: "billing-service synced to 3e0d7a2 · 3 commits by Tom Ellery and Jon Reyes", with the detail line "Indexed by the background sync · cartograph 0.4.0" (who ran it: app, CLI, hook, schedule or MCP). Kinds: index built or failed, sync, correction, rewritten history, stale correction, new breaking contract change. Each row links to a view by hash. Read state moves to the server (POST /api/activity/read) so tabs agree. Toasts are the live half: dismissable by keyboard and announced (already true in Toasts.tsx). No OS notification by default (I5).

Done when

a sync from the CLI raises a toast and an unread row in every open tab; marking read in one tab clears the count in another; ACTIVITY_HREF is gone.

Security

author names and commit subjects are untrusted; render as text, truncate at 120 characters. Activity never includes file contents. "Token about to expire" (D5) doesn't apply while there are no remote connections (R3).

Scale

keep the last 500 rows per workspace; the list pages at 50.

Efficiency

a burst of sync events collapses into one row per repo per run.

Size

M

Tests

unit test for row text from event fixtures; Playwright two-tab test.

SRV-15

MCP over stdio: the six reading tools L

Depends on SRV-03 SRV-08 SRV-09 SRV-11

cartograph mcp on the official Rust SDK (rmcp), stdio transport.

Done when: the MCP Inspector lists eight tools (two still stubbed) and each reading tool returns a result that validates against its outputSchema.

Security
Tool descriptions, instructions and resource text are compile-time constants (F8).
Scale
start-up to the first tool result under 300 ms on a 412 MB index.
Efficiency
the adjacency from SRV-11 is built on first use, not at start-up.
Full text of SRV-15

Depends on (as written)

SRV-03, SRV-08, SRV-09, SRV-11.

What to build

  • cartograph mcp on the official Rust SDK (rmcp), stdio transport. Logs go to stderr and to logs/; stdout carries protocol frames only.
  • The workspace comes from the registry by the process's working folder, or from client roots when given (spec §4).
  • A tool registry: name, description, inputSchema, outputSchema, annotations and a handler that calls the query layer. HTTP GET /api/v1/mcp/tools and mcp --list read the same registry.
  • Tools status, find, describe, neighbors, path and impact, with the schemas in 4.6. All carry readOnlyHint: true.
  • Two renderers per tool: compact Markdown for content, full JSON for structuredContent.
  • The output budget (4.6): rows are dropped from the end until the result fits, and truncated and next_cursor are set.
  • The freshness block on every result, and a call to ensure_fresh (D1).
  • Server instructions: a fixed string under 4,096 characters. The cartograph://status resource.

Done when

the MCP Inspector lists eight tools (two still stubbed) and each reading tool returns a result that validates against its outputSchema. A test asserts that the instructions are under 4,096 characters and that no result exceeds 10k tokens at limit: 50 on the 60k-file fixture.

Security

  • Tool descriptions, instructions and resource text are compile-time constants (F8).
  • Repo-derived strings appear only inside untrusted_text objects, and only when include asks for them.
  • No tool runs code or uses the network.
  • Input is validated against the schema; unknown properties are rejected.

Scale

start-up to the first tool result under 300 ms on a 412 MB index. Several MCP processes may read at once (one per editor window).

Efficiency

the adjacency from SRV-11 is built on first use, not at start-up.

Size

L.

Tests

golden transcripts per tool, schema validation, a budget test, an injection test (a fixture repo whose README says "ignore previous instructions" never appears outside untrusted_text).

What to build

Playwright runs the real binary on a golden index. Matrix: Chromium, Firefox, WebKit; themes light, dark, and dark with More contrast; reduced motion on; viewports 1440, 820 and 390. Each route gets a smoke test, an axe scan and a screenshot. Screenshots run only in one pinned container image (fonts and GPU differ across hosts), with WebGL on SwiftShader and animations off. The canvas is compared with a 0.5% pixel tolerance; everything else exactly. Screenshots are opt-in in CI (decided in Phase 3): pixel-exact baselines needed a Docker retake on every UI change and failed mostly on host differences, while the journeys already cover behaviour. Journeys: first run to map, add workspace, sync while open, correct a link, settings save and conflict, ask and stop, share link, table-only keyboard journey.

IDX-26

Git hooks M

Depends on IDX-15

back ends for hooks install | remove | status (D3)

Security
hooks execute code on every commit, so the installed line uses the absolute path of the binary, quoted, and nothing from the repo.
Scale
43 repos installed in one command.
Efficiency
the hook path does the D2 lock try and exits; no index open.
Full text of IDX-26

Depends on (as written)

IDX-15.

What to build

back ends for hooks install | remove | status (D3):

  • Git 2.54 or later: hook.cartograph.command and hook.cartograph.event entries for post-commit, post-merge, post-checkout, post-rewrite in the repo's config.
  • Older git: find the folder with git rev-parse --git-path hooks (honours core.hooksPath); create the hook if absent; append a block between # cartograph:start and # cartograph:end to an existing shell hook; otherwise refuse and print the line.
  • Husky or lefthook detected: write nothing, print the snippet.
  • The hook body runs cartograph sync --quiet --background; that entry point returns at once when post-checkout reports a file checkout (third argument 0) or a rebase is in progress (rebase-merge or rebase-apply exists).

Done when

on git 2.54 and on an older git, a commit triggers one background sync and git commit returns without waiting (measured overhead under 30 ms); remove restores the exact previous hook bytes; a husky repo is left untouched.

Security

hooks execute code on every commit, so the installed line uses the absolute path of the binary, quoted, and nothing from the repo. Never set core.hooksPath, never overwrite a hook. Existing hook files are edited only when they are regular files inside the hooks folder (no symlink following). status reports a stale binary path.

Scale

43 repos installed in one command.

Efficiency

the hook path does the D2 lock try and exits; no index open.

Size

M.

Tests

matrix test over git versions in CI containers; idempotency (install twice is one block); golden hook files; a rebase of 20 commits triggers zero syncs until it ends.

Full text of IDX-27

Depends on (as written)

IDX-14, IDX-11.

What to build

back ends for schedule hourly | off | status (D4): one user-level job running cartograph sync --fetch --quiet --all-workspaces at a random minute chosen at install: a launchd agent plist on macOS, a systemd user timer with Persistent=true on Linux (crontab when there is no systemd), schtasks on Windows. The job loops over the registry. doctor compares the job's stored binary path with current_exe().

Done when

on each OS the job is listed by the native tool, runs sync --fetch for every registry workspace, and off removes it completely; a moved binary is reported by doctor.

Security

this is the only unattended network use, and it is opt-in. The job file is user-level, never system-level, and never asks for elevation. Fetch uses the user's existing git credentials through git itself; Cartograph stores none. A fetch failure for one repo doesn't stop the loop and never retries in a tight loop.

Scale

tens of workspaces; repos fetched four at a time (proposed).

Efficiency

skips a workspace whose writer lock is held (the dirty flag covers it).

Size

M.

Tests

golden plist, unit and timer files, and schtasks arguments; an end-to-end test per OS in CI where the scheduler is available; a test with one unreachable remote.

Phase 4 · Cross-service links, contracts and corrections

27 items · 4S 14M 9L · roadmap stage 4
Goal

HTTP, event and table links between services, each with evidence and a level; contracts; config and environment resolution; corrections that survive renames; rules; coverage; and the views that read them.

What a user can do at the end

Click a link between two services and read why it exists: the contract operation, the call site, the handler, and the commit that opened it. Correct a wrong link in the app and see cartograph.overrides.yaml change in git diff. cartograph check fails CI on a stale correction or a rule violation.

Exit gate
  • Precision per level published. Proposed gates on the hand-labelled set: exact at or above 0.95, likely at or above 0.75.
  • A correction on a link whose caller file is then renamed still applies; append then delete leaves the override file byte-identical.
  • No planted secret survives in any text column of the index.
  • Cross-service resolution adds at most 20% to a full replay, and at most 50 ms to the 100-commit sync.
  • A subprocess adapter can't open a socket or write a file on macOS and Linux; a fresh clone never runs one unapproved.
Risks retired
  • Config resolution deciding the headline coverage number (XS-09).
  • Third-party adapters as code execution.
  • Printing Exact on evidence nobody measured.

XS-02's service detection is merged into IDX-20; XS-02 now delivers host aliases. Phases 5 and 6 both start from here and can run side by side.

XS-01

Interface fact and predicate registry S

Depends on IDX-03 IDX-05

every string field has a length cap in the type (path 4 KiB, attr value 2 KiB, evidence text 200 chars).
Scale

Efficiency
facts stored as one MessagePack or JSON blob per (blob_id, extractor) row, not one row per fact, so the cache is one read per blob.

XS-03

Query-adapter runtime and the first two adapters M

Depends on XS-01 IDX-05

module adapters. An adapter is a folder: adapter.toml (name, version, language, files globs, requires import hints) plus queries/*.scm.

Done when: on a two-service fixture, the Express route and the fetch call each produce one fact with the right span, and the facts are served from the cache on the second run (0 parses).

Security
.scm is data, but a hostile query can explode: set QueryCursor::set_match_limit (64k), a per-blob time budget (250 ms, via the cursor's progress callback), and a 1 MB input cap…
Scale
remotion: ~105k distinct parses. Adapter queries must add < 15% to parse time per blob.
Efficiency
reuse the tree parsed for imports; run all of a language's queries in one cursor pass; skip an adapter when requires finds no matching import in the file.
Full text of XS-03

Depends on (as written)

XS-01; IDX-05 tree-sitter parse per blob (H1).

What to build

module adapters. An adapter is a folder: adapter.toml (name, version, language, files globs, requires import hints) plus queries/*.scm. Capture names are the contract (4.4). Built-in adapters use the same format and are embedded with include_dir!. Supported predicates: #eq?, #not-eq?, #any-of?, #match? (compiled with the regex crate), #set! for constant attrs. A post-pass turns template strings and concatenations into {parts: [lit|expr]} so the matcher can see which segments are literal. First adapters: express (routes, including router.use(prefix, …) mounts) and fetch (fetch, axios, got: method, URL parts, base expression).

Done when

on a two-service fixture, the Express route and the fetch call each produce one fact with the right span, and the facts are served from the cache on the second run (0 parses).

Security

.scm is data, but a hostile query can explode: set QueryCursor::set_match_limit (64k), a per-blob time budget (250 ms, via the cursor's progress callback), and a 1 MB input cap (cli-and-mcp §8). #match? patterns go through regex with size_limit 1 MiB: linear time, no backtracking. Third-party grammars are never loaded: a query can only target grammars compiled into the binary.

Scale

remotion: ~105k distinct parses. Adapter queries must add < 15% to parse time per blob.

Efficiency

reuse the tree parsed for imports; run all of a language's queries in one cursor pass; skip an adapter when requires finds no matching import in the file.

Size

M

Tests

golden (source file → facts) per adapter; fuzz the .scm loader and the predicate parser; a timeout test with a pathological query.

Full text of XS-04

Depends on (as written)

XS-01–03; IDX-06 replay with a resolver hook (4.1).

What to build

module links::http. Normaliser: lower-case the method; strip scheme, host, query and fragment; collapse //; drop a trailing slash; rewrite :id, {id}, <int:id>, [id], ${expr}, %s to {}; catch-alls (*, [...slug], {path:.*}) to {*}. Route mount prefixes are joined in. Index: per service, a segment trie keyed by method. Match outcomes: full (same segment count, every literal equal), partial (suffix match when the caller's prefix is unknown, or a literal against {*}), none. HttpResolver implements LinkResolver: it keeps a reverse index (method, first literal segment) → call sites and routes, so a commit re-resolves only the keys its changed facts touch. Target service comes from the host: literal host → host_alias. Without a host, a full match that is unique across the workspace is kept; more than 3 candidates becomes an unresolved row with reason ambiguous.

Done when

the fixture's fetch("http://orders:8080/orders/" + id) links to GET /orders/:id in orders, with the commit that opened it; deleting the route closes the interval at that commit; a from-scratch index at 10 sampled commits equals the replayed one.

Security

route patterns that embed a regex (Express, Django re_path) are never executed: they're treated as {*}. Segment count capped at 64, template length at 2 KiB.

Scale

acme: 240 HTTP entrypoints, a few thousand call sites; design for 20k routes and 100k call sites. Lookup is O(segments).

Efficiency

tries live in memory during a run and are rebuilt from facts; only intervals are stored. The reverse index mirrors the research result (60,039 resolutions instead of 37.8M).

Size

M

Tests

property (normalise is idempotent; a template matches itself; replay equals from-scratch); golden table of 200 template pairs across frameworks; fuzz the normaliser.

XS-05

Confidence engine (H2) S

Depends on XS-01 XS-04

module links::confidence. EvidenceType registry: each type has a fixed class and a fixed weight (4.5).

Done when: unit tests reproduce the H2 examples: contract matched on both ends → exact; literal call plus env-resolved host → likely; partial path → guessed; adding any evidence type never moves a link across a band.

Security
none beyond input bounds; evidence count per link capped at 32.
Scale
200k links scored in < 100 ms (pure arithmetic).
Efficiency
score is computed when a link opens or its evidence set changes, never at read time.
Full text of XS-05

Depends on (as written)

XS-01, XS-04.

What to build

module links::confidence. EvidenceType registry: each type has a fixed class and a fixed weight (4.5). score(evidence) → Confidence: class = the weakest class among the chain steps (call-site literalness, base resolution, target anchoring); score = 1 − ∏(1 − wᵢ) over evidence of that class, clamped to the class band (exact 0.90–1.00, likely 0.60–0.89, guessed 0.30–0.59). A fourth internal class weak (0.05–0.29) covers stored-but-hidden links; H2 doesn't say what produces them (3.2). Each link stores level, score, layer (the precedence layer that won) and its evidence rows. Weights live in one weights.toml compiled into the binary and versioned as part of the extractor version, so a re-weight invalidates only link scores (B6).

Done when

unit tests reproduce the H2 examples: contract matched on both ends → exact; literal call plus env-resolved host → likely; partial path → guessed; adding any evidence type never moves a link across a band.

Security

none beyond input bounds; evidence count per link capped at 32.

Scale

200k links scored in < 100 ms (pure arithmetic).

Efficiency

score is computed when a link opens or its evidence set changes, never at read time.

Size

S

Tests

property (score always inside its band; monotone in evidence; order independent); golden for the weight table.

XS-08

OpenAPI and Swagger ingestion, binding and drift L

Depends on XS-01 XS-04 XS-05

module contracts::openapi. Discovery: files named openapi.{yaml,yml,json}, swagger.{yaml,json}, or any YAML/JSON whose root has openapi: or swagger: "2.0"; plus contracts: globs in the manifest (board S5 "Add spec"…

Security
huge and hostile specs: 16 MiB cap for spec files (proposed; the general cap is 1 MB), YAML alias-expansion limit, depth 64, at most 20k operations and 50k schemas per spec, $ref…
Scale
Stripe-sized specs (about 5 MB, 500+ operations) parse in < 2 s; acme has 219 operations.

Full text of XS-08

Depends on (as written)

XS-01, XS-04, XS-05.

What to build

module contracts::openapi. Discovery: files named openapi.{yaml,yml,json}, swagger.{yaml,json}, or any YAML/JSON whose root has openapi: or swagger: "2.0"; plus contracts: globs in the manifest (board S5 "Add spec" writes one). Parse 3.0, 3.1 and 2.0 into one model: Contract, Operation(method, template, operationId), Schema, SchemaField. Base paths from servers[].url and Swagger basePath. $ref: same-file and relative-file refs inside the repo at the same commit; remote refs are never fetched and become unresolved. Binding: operation ↔ parsed route by template (handled_by), call site ↔ operation (consumes). Drift per operation: sync, spec-only, undocumented (a route with no operation). The contract layer outranks parsed in precedence. Owning service: the service whose root contains the spec, or contracts[].service in the manifest.

Done when

the Contracts view on a real fixture shows the same four counts as a hand count; a call that matches a spec operation which also matches the handler's parsed route is exact with three evidence rows.

Security

huge and hostile specs: 16 MiB cap for spec files (proposed; the general cap is 1 MB), YAML alias-expansion limit, depth 64, at most 20k operations and 50k schemas per spec, $ref cycle detection, 5 s budget per spec. Relative $ref paths are normalised and confined to the repo tree at that commit: they're resolved against git trees, never the file system. Descriptions and examples in a spec are repo text: not stored, apart from operation summaries capped at 200 chars and marked untrusted.

Scale

Stripe-sized specs (about 5 MB, 500+ operations) parse in < 2 s; acme has 219 operations.

Efficiency

a spec is parsed once per blob id; a multi-file spec's key is the hash of the root and every referenced blob id (B6).

Size

L

Tests

golden against a corpus of public specs (Petstore 2.0/3.0/3.1, GitHub, Stripe); fuzz the parser and the $ref resolver; property (3.0 converted from 2.0 gives the same operations).
