Full text of XS-09

Depends on (as written)

XS-02, XS-04, XS-05; IDX-12 merged timeline (values live in other repos).

What to build

module links::config. Facts: env.read (process.env.X, os.Getenv, os.environ, env()), client.base (a client built from an env key, so calls on it inherit the base). Value sources, each parsed as a blob fact: .env.example and .env.<name>.example, compose environment:, k8s ConfigMap and container env, Helm values*.yaml. Helm templates aren't rendered: only direct {{ .Values.a.b }} lookups are substituted; anything else is unresolved. Environments: environments: in the manifest maps a name to source globs; defaults are inferred from file names (values-prod.yaml, overlays/staging/, compose and .env.example → local). Tables environment, config_value (4.3). Resolution chain: call site → key → value per env → host → host_alias → service → operation. Each link carries an envs bitmask; when environments resolve to different targets, there is one link per target. Query parameter env; the default is default_environment in the manifest, else prod, else the first. "Save values" writes environments.<env>.set.<KEY> to the manifest. A key with no value yields unresolved rows with reason no value for KEY in <env>, which drives S5's "38 call sites" and the "would raise that to 82%" figure.

Done when

on the fixture, a call through CATALOG_URL is likely in prod and local with different hosts and the same service; removing the Helm value at a commit closes the link at that commit; switching env in the app changes the resolved host column.

Security

see XS-10: a value is stored only when it classifies as a locator. k8s Secret objects and secretKeyRef contribute key names only. Real .env files are ignored even when committed; only *.example, *.sample, *.template are read. No cluster or network access (3.1).

Scale

3 environments × a few hundred keys × history. Bitmask allows 32 environments.

Efficiency

values are facts by blob id; a values-file change re-resolves only call sites that read the changed keys (reverse index key → call sites).

Size

L

Tests

golden per source format; property (replay equals from-scratch across a values change); end to end for the S5 table.

XS-10

Secret hygiene for config values, evidence text and raw references M

Depends on XS-01

module secrets. (1) A locator classifier: a config value is stored only if it parses as a URL, host[:port], or a short identifier (topic, queue, table), and its key doesn't match a deny list…

Done when: a fixture with postgres://user:pw@db/app, an AWS key in .env.example and a JWT in a test file yields no stored secret: checked by scanning every text column of the index for the planted strings.

Merge note: One secrets module for both plans. AI-19 adds only the outbound prompt gate and the hook that feeds docstrings to search.

Security
this item is the mitigation. The rule file has no backtracking engine behind it (regex crate).
Scale
scanning is per blob fact at extract time: 28,940 blobs, under 1 ms each.
Efficiency
one RegexSet pass per string; the entropy check runs only on tokens of 20+ chars.
Full text of XS-10

Depends on (as written)

XS-01. Shared with AI-19's retrieval scan (board S6): one rule set, one module.

What to build

module secrets. (1) A locator classifier: a config value is stored only if it parses as a URL, host[:port], or a short identifier (topic, queue, table), and its key doesn't match a deny list (SECRET|TOKEN|PASSWORD|PASSWD|KEY|CREDENTIAL|PRIVATE|DSN|AUTH). URLs are stored without userinfo, query or fragment. Anything else is stored as value_kind = opaque with no value. (2) A scanner with gitleaks-style rules compiled into one RegexSet, plus a Shannon-entropy check, applied to evidence snippets, unresolved.raw, operation summaries and correction reasons before they leave the query layer; matches are replaced by a mask. Custom rules in the manifest (secrets.rules). Board S6's hit list reads from a secret_hit(path, rule, first_commit) table that stores the masked form only.

Done when

a fixture with postgres://user:pw@db/app, an AWS key in .env.example and a JWT in a test file yields no stored secret: checked by scanning every text column of the index for the planted strings.

Security

this item is the mitigation. The rule file has no backtracking engine behind it (regex crate). User rules are size-limited and compile-checked at manifest load.

Scale

scanning is per blob fact at extract time: 28,940 blobs, under 1 ms each.

Efficiency

one RegexSet pass per string; the entropy check runs only on tokens of 20+ chars.

Size

M

Tests

golden against gitleaks' own test vectors; property (no planted secret survives in any index column); fuzz the URL scrubber.

Full text of XS-12

Depends on (as written)

XS-04, XS-05; IDX-18 lineage ids; IDX-03 format versioning (B3); SRV-01.

What to build

module corrections. Rust types for cartograph.overrides.yaml (4.7) with version: 1 and a schemars JSON Schema. Key grammar: kind plus from, to and one of op, topic, table; an end is service or service:path or service:path#symbol. Binding: each entry is resolved to lineage ids as of an anchor commit, which is the commit that added the entry to the overrides file (found from the file's history; an uncommitted entry anchors at HEAD). The binding is stored in table correction (4.3) and followed forward through renames. Apply pass, after link resolution and before derived data: remove hides matching links, retarget rewrites the far end, set fixes the level (score = band midpoint: 0.95, 0.75, 0.45), add opens a link over the interval in which both ends exist. Affected links get layer = correction and keep the base values for the inspector and for undo. Status per entry: bound, stale (matches nothing at HEAD), ambiguous (matches more than one target for a retarget). cartograph check: exit 1 when any entry is stale and stale: error (default), a warning line under warn, silent under off; --json prints {schema:1, corrections:[…], rules:[…]}. by and at in the UI come from the file's blame.

Done when

a correction on a link whose caller file is then renamed still applies; deleting the handler makes check exit 1 naming the entry and its line; stale: warn turns that into exit 0.

Security

the file is committed by teammates, so it's untrusted: reason is data, capped at 500 chars, never placed in tool descriptions or instructions, and returned through MCP only as untrusted_text (F8). A correction can't name a path outside the workspace: ends resolve only to indexed nodes. Entry count capped at 10k.

Scale

1,000 corrections against 200k links: binding and apply < 200 ms, by index lookup on the natural key, not a scan.

Efficiency

the apply pass runs without re-parsing; its inputs_key is the overrides blob hash, so an unchanged file costs nothing (B6).

Size

L

Tests

golden (file + index → effective links); property (apply is idempotent; remove then undo restores the base link; binding is stable under a rename chain); fuzz the YAML reader; end to end for check exit codes.

as XS-03.

XS-19

Spec versions over history and breaking changes M

Depends on XS-08 IDX-18

table contract_version(contract_id, n, commit, spec_hash, breaking).

Done when: on a fixture history with three spec edits, the view lists v1–v3 with the right commit each, flags the one removal as breaking, and lists the operation's callers.

Security
diffing is bounded by the caps of XS-08; a spec that flips between huge versions is rate-limited by the per-blob budget.
Scale
a spec edited 500 times over 9.5k commits: 500 parses, cached by blob id; diff of two 500-operation specs < 50 ms.
Efficiency
store only the breaking-change list and counts per version, not full diffs; recompute a full diff on request.
Full text of XS-19

Depends on (as written)

XS-08; IDX-18 lineage for the spec file.

What to build

table contract_version(contract_id, n, commit, spec_hash, breaking). A new version is recorded whenever the canonical spec hash changes along first-parent history; the file is followed across renames. A structural diff classifies changes; breaking = removed operation, removed response field, new required request field or parameter, narrowed type, removed enum value (a subset of oasdiff's rules, reimplemented). For each breaking change, list the callers of the affected operation at that commit. Field-level "still reads it" is XS-29. Summary strings ("Added POST /v1/…") are generated from the diff, not by a model.

Done when

on a fixture history with three spec edits, the view lists v1–v3 with the right commit each, flags the one removal as breaking, and lists the operation's callers.

Security

diffing is bounded by the caps of XS-08; a spec that flips between huge versions is rate-limited by the per-blob budget.

Scale

a spec edited 500 times over 9.5k commits: 500 parses, cached by blob id; diff of two 500-operation specs < 50 ms.

Efficiency

store only the breaking-change list and counts per version, not full diffs; recompute a full diff on request.

Size

M

Tests

golden diff pairs; property (diff(a, a) is empty; added-then-removed is breaking exactly once).

Consumers = links into the entrypoint's operation or channel, each with its level, plus external clients declared in the manifest (clients:) and attached by add corrections, shown with an "override" chip. Reach: a bounded BFS over the links valid at the date, run at query time on an in-memory adjacency for that snapshot; only the HEAD count per entrypoint ("Symbols reached") is stored as a derived row. XS-22

Architecture rules, baseline and the rules side of check (H6) M

Depends on XS-05 XS-12 XS-23

module rules. Types for rules: in the manifest (4.7). Selectors

Done when: the H6 example rule fails check on a fixture where a layer:web service reads a table, passes once the violation is baselined, and never fails on a guessed link.

Security
path regexes compile with the regex crate under a size limit, so a hostile manifest can't cause catastrophic backtracking.
Scale
100 rules × 200k links < 2 s: selectors are pre-resolved to service and tag sets, so each rule is an indexed filter, not a scan.
Efficiency
violations at HEAD are derived rows keyed by rule hash and link set hash (B6).
Full text of XS-22

Depends on (as written)

XS-12 (check skeleton), XS-05, XS-23 for owner tags.

What to build

module rules. Types for rules: in the manifest (4.7). Selectors: tags, service, path (regex with $1 captures usable in the other end), kind. Tags come from services[].tags in the manifest plus automatic domain:, lang:, owner: (unspecified; proposed). Rule types: forbid (in H6); no_cycles proposed as a second type since X11 names it. A violation is a link at or above min_confidence (default likely) matching the rule; it has the link's interval, so pass or fail per commit is a lookup. Baseline cartograph.baseline.json: {version: 1, violations: [{rule, kind, from, to, op}]} in natural keys; check --update-baseline rewrites it; a baselined violation doesn't fail; a baseline entry that no longer matches is reported as fixed. GET /api/rules, GET /api/rules/violations. Severity error fails check with exit 1; warn and info print.

Done when

the H6 example rule fails check on a fixture where a layer:web service reads a table, passes once the violation is baselined, and never fails on a guessed link.

Security

path regexes compile with the regex crate under a size limit, so a hostile manifest can't cause catastrophic backtracking. Rule and baseline counts capped (1k rules, 100k baseline entries).

Scale

100 rules × 200k links < 2 s: selectors are pre-resolved to service and tag sets, so each rule is an indexed filter, not a scan.

Efficiency

violations at HEAD are derived rows keyed by rule hash and link set hash (B6).

Size

M

Tests

golden; property (baseline of all current violations makes check pass; rule order doesn't matter); fuzz the selector parser.

XS-24

Subprocess adapters: NDJSON protocol, trust and sandbox (H4b) L

Depends on XS-01 XS-03 IDX-11 SRV-01

module adapters::subprocess. adapter.toml with command, args, files globs, version.

Done when: a sample Python adapter produces facts that appear as links; a fresh clone with the same manifest indexes without running it and says why; a line that names another file's path is rejected.

Security
this is the main code-execution risk in the area: a manifest in a cloned repo names a command.
Scale
process start cost is paid once per run; throughput target 2k blobs/s for a trivial adapter.
Efficiency
pipelined requests with a window of 32; content skipped for blobs already cached.
Full text of XS-24

Depends on (as written)

XS-01, XS-03; IDX-11 registry (C4); SRV-01.

What to build

module adapters::subprocess. adapter.toml with command, args, files globs, version. Protocol in 4.4: Cartograph starts one process per run, sends one extract request per blob on stdin with the content inline, and reads fact lines until a done line. Facts are cached by (blob_id, name, version), so an adapter runs once per distinct blob. Trust: an adapter declared in a committed manifest doesn't run until the user approves it with cartograph adapters allow <name>, which stores a hash of adapter.toml and the resolved command path in the per-user registry; a changed hash asks again. cartograph adapters list|allow|deny. The local guide (a static page in the binary) and the GitHub issue link behind "Request an adapter".

Done when

a sample Python adapter produces facts that appear as links; a fresh clone with the same manifest indexes without running it and says why; a line that names another file's path is rejected.

Security

this is the main code-execution risk in the area: a manifest in a cloned repo names a command. Mitigations: (1) the allow list above, kept outside any repo; MCP sync and the background refresh never approve. (2) Sandbox: macOS sandbox-exec profile, Linux bubblewrap or landlock + seccomp with no network namespace, both denying network and all file writes and all reads outside the adapter's own folder. If no sandbox is available (Windows at first: job-object limits only), the adapter is refused unless the user passes --unsandboxed-adapters. (3) Content arrives on stdin, so the process needs no repo access. (4) Environment scrubbed (no CARTOGRAPH_AI_KEY, minimal PATH), empty temp working directory. (5) Limits: 10 s wall per blob, CPU and address-space rlimits, 4 MiB output per blob, 64 KiB per line, 10k facts per blob; a breach kills the process and records an unresolved row. (6) Every fact is validated: schema, repo/commit/path must echo the request, span inside the file, kind in the registry, evidence text capped and secret-masked.

Scale

process start cost is paid once per run; throughput target 2k blobs/s for a trivial adapter.

Efficiency

pipelined requests with a window of 32; content skipped for blobs already cached.

Size

L

Tests

end to end with a sample adapter; fuzz the NDJSON reader; fault injection (adapter hangs, floods, exits mid-line, lies about the path); a test that network and file writes fail inside the sandbox on each OS.

Phase 5 · Symbols, branch overlays and impact

8 items · 0S 3M 5L · roadmap stage 5
Goal

Nodes below file level and the calls between them, branch overlays and working-tree facts, hunks, commit impact for a ref, SCIP in and out, concepts.

What a user can do at the end

Open a file's focus view and see its functions with callers and callees. Run cartograph impact <ref> on a pull-request branch and get the same answer as the Impact view, grouped as exact, likely and guessed.

Exit gate
  • Main as of the merge base plus the overlay equals a from-scratch index of the branch head.
  • impact --working-tree leaves the index and the cache byte-identical.
  • Before building call replay: measure on remotion. If call intervals more than double the index, store definitions and references only and resolve calls on request.
  • SCIP export then import is a no-op on the fact hash; agreement with scip-typescript on at least 90% of in-repo call edges (proposed).
Risks retired
  • Call links dominating index size.
  • The one place the indexer reads checked-out files (traversal, symlinks).

XS-21 and XS-26 named each other as a dependency. The cycle is broken here: hunks map to symbols first (XS-26), then commit impact reads them (XS-21).

IDX-24

Branch overlays and working-tree facts L

Depends on IDX-14 IDX-18

overlay(repo, ref) -> BranchId: find the merge base of ref with the indexed branch on its first-parent chain; if the base is past the indexed commit, sync first.

Done when: for a branch fixture, "main as of base plus overlay" equals a from-scratch index of the branch head; after the branch merges, the next sync drops the overlay and main holds the links; working_tree_delta on a dirty fixture…

Security
impact <ref> takes a user- or agent-supplied ref; it is resolved with gix rev-parse inside a registry repo only, never passed to a shell.
Scale
an overlay costs about its diff (B5): a 50-commit PR touching 200 files is a sub-second replay.
Efficiency
state as of the base is built from the head state by undoing steps when the base is near the head, and by an as-of scan otherwise.
Full text of IDX-24

Depends on (as written)

IDX-14, IDX-18.

What to build

  • overlay(repo, ref) -> BranchId: find the merge base of ref with the indexed branch on its first-parent chain; if the base is past the indexed commit, sync first. Rehydrate the replay state as of the base seq; replay the commits from base to the branch head; write branch, overlay_commit and overlay_interval rows (§4.3). An overlay row either opens a link or masks a main link, which is how "the overlay rows win" (B5) is represented.
  • Created for the checked-out branch when it differs from the indexed one, and for impact <ref>. Updated incrementally when the branch moves; rebuilt when its base moves.
  • Dropped when the ref is deleted or its head is an ancestor of the indexed branch; plus a least-recently-used cap of 20 overlays per repo (proposed).
  • working_tree_delta(repo) -> Delta: read modified and untracked source files (respecting .gitignore and excludes), extract facts in memory, apply as one virtual step on top of HEAD's state, and return opened and closed links. Nothing is written (spec §5).

Done when

for a branch fixture, "main as of base plus overlay" equals a from-scratch index of the branch head; after the branch merges, the next sync drops the overlay and main holds the links; working_tree_delta on a dirty fixture reports the right links and leaves the index and cache byte-identical.

Security

impact <ref> takes a user- or agent-supplied ref; it is resolved with gix rev-parse inside a registry repo only, never passed to a shell. Working-tree reads are confined to the canonical repo root, don't follow symlinks out of it, apply the 1 MB cap and a 5,000-file cap (proposed), and are the only place the indexer reads checked-out files, so that code path gets its own fuzz and traversal tests.

Scale

an overlay costs about its diff (B5): a 50-commit PR touching 200 files is a sub-second replay. Rehydration as of a past seq reads file_version by range, at 60k files.

Efficiency

state as of the base is built from the head state by undoing steps when the base is near the head, and by an as-of scan otherwise. Facts come from the shared cache.

Size

L.

Tests

oracle test (overlay equals from-scratch at the branch head); lifecycle tests (create, move, rebase, merge, delete); a no-write assertion for the working-tree path; traversal tests with symlinks pointing outside the repo.

What to build

module symbols. Per-blob facts from tree-sitter tags.scm-style queries: symbol.def (name, kind, span, signature span, exported) and symbol.ref (name, role call or type use, span, receiver or import binding). Symbol identity: a SCIP-style moniker (cartograph <manager> <package> <version> <descriptor>), with file lineage so a moved file keeps its symbols. Resolution of a ref: same-file definition → import binding to a unique exported definition in the resolved file (exact) → a unique exported name in the same package (likely) → name-only (guessed, hidden from the focus view by default). Call links have intervals and are resolved with the same reverse-index replay (key = symbol name). Queries symbols_in(file, at) and symbol_neighbors(id, at) returning callers and callees grouped by file with counts, the first 3 files expanded (board G11). GET /api/files/{id}/symbols, GET /api/symbols/{id}. find gains kind: symbol.

Done when

the focus view on a real TS file lists its functions with callers and callees matching a hand count on the fixture, and SCIP data (XS-27) agrees on at least 90% of in-repo call edges for the TS fixture (proposed bar; unspecified).

Security

signatures and bodies are repo text, read on demand by span and returned as untrusted text. Model-written summaries belong to AI-21.

Scale

60k files × about 10 symbols = 600k symbols, a few million references. Before building replay for calls, measure on remotion: if call intervals more than double the index (412 MB example), store only defs and refs as blob facts and resolve calls on request for the focus view and for impact (3.2).

Efficiency

defs and refs come from the tree already parsed for imports; ref facts are delta-friendly since most blobs don't change.

Size

L

Tests

golden per language; property (replay equals from-scratch at sampled commits); the SCIP agreement check.

Full text of XS-21

Depends on (as written)

XS-20; IDX-24 branch overlays (B5) and in-memory parse of uncommitted files; XS-26 for symbol detail.

What to build

query commit_impact(sha): links opened and closed at that commit (indexed on valid_from/valid_to), the count that are cross-service, entrypoints whose reach differs between the commit and its first parent (with the size of the difference), contract versions created at it, files touched with line counts, and prev/next along the first-parent chain or the open overlay (I9). Query impact(input): input is paths, a ref, the working tree or since; output is affected nodes across services grouped as exact, likely, guessed, with the path that reaches each. GET /api/commits/{sha}/impact, POST /api/impact, cartograph impact, MCP impact (4.8). Markdown renderer for --format md (D11).

Done when

on the fixture, the commit that swaps a table read for an HTTP call reports one link removed, two added, one new cross-service link and two entrypoints with new reach; impact --working-tree reflects an unsaved-to-git edit without changing the index's fact hash.

Security

ref is resolved through gix, never passed to a shell. Paths are normalised and must be inside a registered repo. Commit messages are untrusted text. Working-tree content is never written to the index (cli-and-mcp §5).

Scale

per-commit answer < 50 ms; since over 1,000 commits is a range scan on intervals, not 1,000 queries.

Efficiency

reach deltas use two BFS runs sharing the snapshot cache; MCP output is capped at 50 rows per level with a cursor (F2).

Size

L

Tests

Efficiency

Full text of XS-27

Depends on (as written)

XS-25.

What to build

module scip using the scip crate. cartograph scip import <file> --repo <name> [--commit <sha>] (default: the indexed HEAD): each document's occurrences are attached to the blob at that path and commit as facts from extractor scip:<tool name>, so they stay valid wherever that blob persists; references resolve by SCIP symbol string and rank above tree-sitter results (evidence scip_precise, exact). cartograph scip export --repo <name> [--at <commit>] -o index.scip writes documents, occurrences and symbol information from the index. These two commands aren't in the CLI spec (3.2).

Done when

importing scip-typescript output for the fixture upgrades guessed and likely call links to exact; an exported file loads in the scip CLI (scip print) without errors; export then import is a no-op on the fact hash.

Security

a SCIP file is untrusted protobuf: 512 MiB size cap, streaming decode by document, relative_path normalised and required to exist in the tree at that commit (no .., no absolute paths); documents for unknown paths are dropped with a count. Nothing in it is executed.

Scale

SCIP indexes for large repos reach hundreds of MB: stream, never load whole.

Efficiency

occurrences stored per blob in the same packed form as tree-sitter refs.

Size

M

Tests

golden round trip; fuzz the importer; compatibility test against scip-typescript and scip-go output.

Scale
tens of thousands of candidate names; grouping by normalised stem is a hash join.
Efficiency
derived rows with inputs_key over member fact hashes.
Full text of XS-28

Depends on (as written)

XS-08, XS-16, XS-17, XS-25. No embeddings (G7).

What to build

module concepts. Candidate names from schema names, table names, topic stems, exported type and class names and doc headings; normalised (case split, singular, suffixes like DTO, Model, Entity, Repo stripped). A concept needs members in two or more layers. Members carry layer (contract, data, event, code, docs), how and a level: equal names across layers → exact; alias or stem match → likely; decided in the file → overridden. Field overlap (Jaccard over field names) scores near matches; 0.5–0.7 with a conflict goes to "needs a decision". Decisions are written to a concepts: section of the overrides file (proposed, 3.2). Domain = majority domain of members. The one-line summary is model-written and optional, owned by AI-21. GET /api/concepts, GET /api/concepts/{id}, POST /api/concepts/{id}/decision.

Done when

on the fixture, Invoice gathers its schema, table, event and types with the right level each, and "Keep separate" persists across a rebuild.

Security

names are repo text but short identifiers; capped at 100 chars. Decisions use the write path of XS-13.

Scale

tens of thousands of candidate names; grouping by normalised stem is a hash join.

Efficiency

derived rows with inputs_key over member fact hashes.

Size

L

Tests

golden; property (grouping is independent of input order); a labelled set in XS-07 for member precision.

Phase 6 · Ask in Rust with checked citations

22 items · 5S 14M 3L · roadmap stage 6
Goal

One agent loop in the binary for the app and cartograph ask: the deterministic engine first, then providers, keys in the keychain, the outbound policy, spotlighting, the four citation rules, history in ask.db, the ultra packer, summaries.

What a user can do at the end

Ask a question in the docked panel with no model set and get the graph engine's answer with links. Save a key once (the field clears and the browser never sees it again), ask again, and read an answer in which every sentence is marked checked or unsupported. Find the turn later in cartograph ask --history.

Exit gate
  • Every planted injection ends as a checked answer, an unsupported flag or the engine fallback; none produces a link, a write or a connection other than to the provider.
  • A canary key appears in no API response, log, report or ask.db.
  • With local_only on, a run against a remote provider fails before any socket opens.
  • Engine answer under 150 ms p95 on the 60k and 200k fixture; packing under 100 ms; Stop frees the run within 1 s.
  • next build with output: "export" has no app/api; no key field in localStorage.
Risks retired
  • genai carrying five provider protocols.
  • Server-side request forgery through a custom base URL.
  • Prompt injection through names, paths, docstrings and commit messages.
  • Rule 4 of G5 flagging correct sentences (measured in AI-22).

AI-02 to AI-05 give a working Ask on real data with no model and no keys. They need only phase 3, so they can start while phase 4 runs.

AI-02

Ask tool adapter and the handle table M

Depends on SRV-11 SRV-15

crate cartograph-ai (or module ai/ in the one crate), with tools.rs.

Done when: calling each tool through the adapter on a fixture index returns the same rows as cartograph tool <name>, plus handles; the table round-trips to JSON matching Handle in lib/ask.ts.

Security
read-only by type: the adapter takes a &ReadSnapshot, which has no write methods.
Scale
60k files, 200k links. A turn's handle table is capped at 2,000 handles; beyond that, rows are dropped by rank and counted in left_out.
Efficiency
one read snapshot per turn, so all tool calls in a turn see one index version.
Full text of AI-02

Depends on (as written)

SRV-02 read functions with an at date; SRV-15 tool schemas (F1).

What to build

crate cartograph-ai (or module ai/ in the one crate), with tools.rs. It wraps the five read tools find, describe, neighbors, path, impact from the same registry MCP uses. status, sync and correct are not registered with Ask (G6). Per turn it holds a HandleTable: every row a tool returns gets e1, e2…, with {kind: node|link|commit|operation, id (lineage id), label, from, to}. A repeated row keeps its first handle. The adapter pins at to the turn's date server-side, so the model cannot pick another date except through impact.since.

Done when

calling each tool through the adapter on a fixture index returns the same rows as cartograph tool <name>, plus handles; the table round-trips to JSON matching Handle in lib/ask.ts.

Security

read-only by type: the adapter takes a &ReadSnapshot, which has no write methods. Tool arguments from the model are validated against the JSON Schema before the call. Row limit 50 and a 10k-token cap per result (F2). Repo text comes back only in untrusted_text fields (F8).

Scale

60k files, 200k links. A turn's handle table is capped at 2,000 handles; beyond that, rows are dropped by rank and counted in left_out.

Efficiency

one read snapshot per turn, so all tool calls in a turn see one index version. Results are concise rows, not prose.

Size

M.

Tests

unit (handle assignment, dedupe, caps); golden (adapter output per tool on the fixture); property (every handle id resolves in the snapshot it came from).

Depends on (as written)

AI-02.

What to build

engine.rs: a port of ask(), impact, path, stories, readingOrder, mentions and since from lib/ask.ts, rewritten as calls through the AI-02 adapter, so every engine sentence already carries handles. Output is the Answer shape (title, lines: Part[][], nodes, links, steps, plan). mentions() becomes an FTS5 exact-name lookup (AI-15; until then, find by name). Done when

the 4 SUGGESTIONS questions and each / tool, run on an index built from the northwind demo, give the same nodes, links and plan as the TS engine (golden files exported from the TS engine first).

Security

the engine never calls a model, so it is the safe floor: with no provider, nothing leaves the machine. Node names in Parts are ids resolved server-side, never free text turned into links.

Scale

impact on a node with fan-in in the thousands: bounded BFS with a depth cap of 6 and a node cap of 5,000, reported as "and N more". path uses bidirectional BFS over the time-sliced adjacency, not a scan of every link per hop as the TS does.

Efficiency

target under 150 ms p95 for any plan on the 60k/200k fixture, so the engine answer still shows "instantly" before the model words it.

Size

M.

Tests

AI-04

Run API: SSE streaming, Stop, limits M

Depends on AI-03 SRV-05 SRV-07

Done when: with no provider configured, a question streams run.started → plan → tool.call/tool.result → answer → done and the UI shows the engine answer; aborting the fetch mid-run frees the task within 1 s (asserted by a counter of live…

Merge note: Owns the run API. Mounted at /api/v1/w/{ws}/ask/runs. Two concurrent runs per process (SRV-19 proposed four per workspace). Whether a dropped connection cancels the run is a call for you (see Rulings).

Security
this is a mutating, spend-causing endpoint in an API that A1 calls read-only (see Risk R1).
Scale
one user, so concurrency is small. The risk is a runaway loop, which the limits bound.
Efficiency

Full text of AI-04

Depends on (as written)

AI-03; SRV-05 routing and the session-token middleware.

What to build

POST /api/ask/runs returning text/event-stream (the client uses fetch streaming, since EventSource cannot POST or send the token header). DELETE /api/ask/runs/{id} to stop. Events are listed in §4.2. Each run is a tokio task with a CancellationToken; a client disconnect or a DELETE cancels it and drops the upstream connection. A stopped run keeps the sentences that arrived and is stored with status: stopped. Heartbeat comment every 15 s.

Done when

with no provider configured, a question streams run.started → plan → tool.call/tool.result → answer → done and the UI shows the engine answer; aborting the fetch mid-run frees the task within 1 s (asserted by a counter of live runs).

Security

this is a mutating, spend-causing endpoint in an API that A1 calls read-only (see Risk R1). It needs the session token, the Origin and Host checks, Content-Type: application/json only (no simple-request CSRF), a 64 KB body cap and a 4,000-character question cap. The request carries no base URL and no key, ever. Limits per run: 6 model round trips, 12 tool calls, 120 s wall clock, 2,000 output tokens. Two concurrent runs per process; a third gets 429.

Scale

one user, so concurrency is small. The risk is a runaway loop, which the limits bound.

Efficiency

events are flushed per sentence, not per token (AI-12), which also cuts the O(n²) re-check the TS does on every chunk.

Size

M.

Tests

end-to-end with a scripted fake model; cancellation and limit tests; fuzz the request body.

Full text of AI-07

Depends on (as written)

AI-01.

What to build

~/.cartograph/providers.toml (next to registry.toml, C4; version = 1, JSON Schema via schemars, B3). Built-ins are compiled in and need no file: anthropic (native), openai, google (native Gemini), xai, ollama. The file adds custom entries and overrides (format in §4.5). ModelClient (§4.6) is the only thing the loop sees; the genai implementation sits behind it, so a provider can be swapped for a direct call if the spike found a gap. Context windows and optional prices live in the registry; for Ollama the window is read from the server (num_ctx), since its default is far below 64k. The AI module is not linked into the mcp command path.

Done when

cartograph ai providers --json lists built-ins and custom entries with key_saved and source; a bad TOML gives a line-and-column error; the fake provider (kind = "fake", test builds only) drives the loop.

Security

auth kinds are api_key and none only. There is no OAuth kind and no code path that opens a login page (G1); a test greps the built binary for claude.ai and oauth. Built-in base URLs are constants and cannot be overridden by the file, so a key saved for anthropic can only go to api.anthropic.com. Supply chain: genai is a young crate; pin the exact version, run cargo-deny and cargo-audit, use rustls.

Scale

tens of providers at most.

Efficiency

one shared reqwest::Client with connection reuse; model lists cached for 10 minutes.

Size

M.

Tests

unit (TOML parsing, schema); golden (the lib/ai.ts presets map to registry entries); contract tests of ModelClient against the fake and, on demand, real providers.

Full text of AI-08

Depends on (as written)

AI-07.

What to build

KeyStore (§4.6) with three backends in order: env (CARTOGRAPH_AI_KEY with CARTOGRAPH_AI_BASE_URL, G4), keychain (keyring; service cartograph, account ai:<provider-id>), and on Linux with no Secret Service a file ~/.cartograph/credentials.toml, mode 0600, refused if its mode is wider. Env set means a locked provider env that the UI shows as "Set by the environment" (board G15) and cannot edit. Per-provider env keys only when the TOML names them (key_env = "OPENAI_API_KEY"); ambient vars are never read on their own. CLI: cartograph ai key set <provider> (reads stdin or a no-echo prompt, never argv), ai key rm, ai test <provider>. Keys are secrecy::SecretString, zeroised on drop, with no Debug. A Redactor holds the live key values and patterns (sk-…, AIza…, xai-…, Bearer …) and is applied to every provider error body, every SSE error event, every log line and every report.

Done when

a key set in the app is readable by cartograph ask; GET /api/ai/providers never contains a key (asserted by a test that sets a canary key and scans every API response, log file and ask.db); a 401 from a provider that echoes the key reaches the browser with the key masked.

Security

threats: a key in a response body, a log, a crash report, shell history, a core dump or ask.db. Mitigations: the canary scan above; stdin-only entry; no key suffix shown (not even the last four characters); the browser gets key_saved and source only. macOS may prompt again after an unsigned binary is replaced; doctor explains it. CARTOGRAPH_AI_API_KEY (the name route.ts uses today) is accepted for one release with a warning (R4).

Scale

n/a.

Efficiency

keychain reads are cached in memory for the process; one read per provider per run at most.

Size

M.

Tests

AI-09

Outbound request policy (SSRF) S

Depends on AI-07

one OutboundPolicy used by every model call and model-list call.

Done when: a table-driven test covers each rule, including a redirect to 169.254.169.254, a hostname that resolves to link-local, and a DNS answer that changes after validation.

Merge note: The one outbound policy. Replaces SRV-19's shorter rule.

Security
this replaces the dev-only switch in route.ts. The residual risk is a user pointing a custom endpoint at a LAN host on purpose, which is their call on a single-user tool.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
the resolver result is reused for the connection pool's lifetime.
Full text of AI-09

Depends on (as written)

AI-07.

What to build

one OutboundPolicy used by every model call and model-list call. Rules:

  1. The base URL comes only from the registry or env, never from a request body.
  2. Scheme https always allowed; http only to loopback, or to a host listed with allow_http = true in the TOML.
  3. No userinfo in URLs. No redirects followed.
  4. Resolve, validate, then connect to the validated address (pin it), so DNS cannot change between check and use.
  5. Always refused: link-local (169.254.0.0/16, fe80::/10, which covers cloud metadata), multicast, unspecified, and Cartograph's own listening port.
  6. Loopback and private ranges are allowed, because Ollama, LM Studio and a team endpoint on a LAN are the normal case.
  7. A key is bound to the origin it was saved for. Changing a custom endpoint's host clears its key and asks again.
  8. Response caps: 1 MB for a model list, 300 redacted characters for an error body. Timeouts: 10 s connect, 60 s idle.

Done when

a table-driven test covers each rule, including a redirect to 169.254.169.254, a hostname that resolves to link-local, and a DNS answer that changes after validation.

Security

this replaces the dev-only switch in route.ts. The residual risk is a user pointing a custom endpoint at a LAN host on purpose, which is their call on a single-user tool. Adding or changing an endpoint is a settings write behind the session token (A3), so a web page cannot do it.

Scale

n/a.

Efficiency

the resolver result is reused for the connection pool's lifetime.

Size

S.

Tests

unit and table-driven; a local test server for redirects.

AI-11

Prompt assembly: spotlighting, datamarking, cache order M

Depends on AI-02 AI-07

prompt.rs. Message order, most stable first, so provider prompt caches hit

Done when: a snapshot test shows the first two blocks are byte-identical across two different questions and two threads; a data string containing </data-…> or "ignore previous instructions" stays inside the delimiters.

Security
G6, F8, X2. Injection can still change the wording; AI-12 catches that.
Scale
prompts up to about 100k tokens at ultra.
Efficiency
with a cached prefix, a follow-up in a thread pays full price only for the new turn.
Full text of AI-11

Depends on (as written)

AI-02, AI-07.

What to build

prompt.rs. Message order, most stable first, so provider prompt caches hit:

  1. System prompt: static, versioned (prompt_version), with no date, no workspace name and no nonce.
  2. Tool definitions: static, sorted by name. Cache breakpoint here (Anthropic cache_control; OpenAI caches the prefix on its own).
  3. Earlier turns of the thread, with their handle markers stripped and replaced by labels, so an old handle cannot be cited again. Second breakpoint at the end of history.
  4. The current turn: the date, the per-thread nonce declaration, the engine's findings and tool rows, then the question last.

Spotlighting: every repo-derived string (names, paths, docstrings, commit messages, summaries, untrusted_text) goes inside <data-NONCE>…</data-NONCE>, where NONCE is 128 random bits per thread (per thread, not per turn, so history still caches). Any occurrence of the nonce in data is removed. Datamarking is applied to free-text fields (docstrings, commit messages, summaries): whitespace is replaced with a marker character the system prompt names. Names and paths are length-capped at 200 characters and stripped of control characters. Tool results are appended, never reordered.

Done when

a snapshot test shows the first two blocks are byte-identical across two different questions and two threads; a data string containing </data-…> or "ignore previous instructions" stays inside the delimiters.

Security

G6, F8, X2. Injection can still change the wording; AI-12 catches that. Summaries written by a model are treated as untrusted too, since their input was repo text.

Scale

prompts up to about 100k tokens at ultra.

Efficiency

with a cached prefix, a follow-up in a thread pays full price only for the new turn. Cached-token counts from usage are recorded (AI-14) so the hit rate is visible.

Size

M.

Tests

golden prompts; property (no data string can close its own delimiter); an injection corpus (AI-22).

AI-12

The citation checker in Rust (all four G5 rules) M

Depends on AI-02 SRV-02

cite.rs, a pure function plus one index lookup. Decision on where it lives

Done when: the Rust checker reproduces the TS checker's output on a golden set for rules 1 and 3, and passes new golden cases for rules 2 and 4 (a sentence naming an uncited file; a relation claimed with only node citations; a handle from…

Merge note: The only citation checker. checkCitations in lib/ask.ts is deleted in AI-05.

Security
deterministic, so it cannot be talked round by injected text (G5 rejects a model judge).
Scale
answers are under 2,000 tokens; the check is microseconds per sentence.
Efficiency
incremental per sentence; one batched existence query per turn in the common case.
Full text of AI-12

Depends on (as written)

AI-02; SRV-02 exists(ids) and intervals.

What to build

cite.rs, a pure function plus one index lookup. Decision on where it lives: Rust only. checkCitations in lib/ask.ts is deleted in AI-05, because G3 says citations are checked in one place. It works incrementally, per completed sentence:

  • Rule 1: the handle is in this turn's table.
  • Rule 2: the object still exists in the current index (one batched lookup per sentence batch; if fact_hash changed mid-turn, every handle is re-validated).
  • Rule 3: its interval covers at.
  • Rule 4 (unspecified in detail; proposed): find identifiers in the sentence by matching against the labels and aliases of this turn's rows (Aho-Corasick over at most 2,000 handles, not over 60k nodes), plus code-shaped tokens (paths, camelCase, snake_case, 7–40 hex characters). Each matched identifier must belong to a cited handle (the node itself, or an end of a cited link). A code-shaped token that matches no row of this turn makes the sentence unsupported. If the sentence names two or more nodes, at least one cited handle must be a link, path or commit that touches two of them; a link between descendants counts for their ancestors.

Failed citations are removed; a sentence with none left is unsupported. Above 30% unsupported: one repair call that lists each failed sentence with the reason; if still above 30%, the engine answer with the existing notice copy from store.ts. Output matches Checked in lib/ask.ts.

Done when

the Rust checker reproduces the TS checker's output on a golden set for rules 1 and 3, and passes new golden cases for rules 2 and 4 (a sentence naming an uncited file; a relation claimed with only node citations; a handle from an earlier turn).

Security

deterministic, so it cannot be talked round by injected text (G5 rejects a model judge). It is the backstop for G6: an injected instruction can at worst produce sentences that fail here.

Scale

answers are under 2,000 tokens; the check is microseconds per sentence.

Efficiency

incremental per sentence; one batched existence query per turn in the common case.

Size

M.

Tests

golden; property (removing a handle from the table never turns an unsupported sentence into a supported one); fuzz the sentence splitter with Unicode and unbalanced brackets.

AI-13

The agent loop L

Depends on AI-03 AI-04 AI-07 AI-08 AI-11 AI-12

agent.rs. Per run: (1) the engine runs first and its rows seed the handle table, so the user sees an answer at once (the behaviour store.ts has today); (2) if a provider is ready, the model gets the findings and the tools…

Done when: with the fake model scripted to call neighbors then answer, the SSE shows the calls, the checked sentences and usage; scripted bad citations trigger exactly one repair; a scripted provider failure yields the engine answer with…

Security
no tool writes, runs code or reaches the network (G6). Tool names from the model are matched against the five-name allow list; unknown names return an error result to the model and…
Scale
worst case 6 round trips at ultra is 6 × 100k input tokens; prompt caching (AI-11) makes rounds 2–6 mostly cached, and a per-run input ceiling (default 400k tokens, in settings) stops…
Efficiency
the engine-first step removes one or two round trips for the four common plans.
Full text of AI-13

Depends on (as written)

AI-03, AI-04, AI-07, AI-08, AI-11, AI-12.

What to build

agent.rs. Per run: (1) the engine runs first and its rows seed the handle table, so the user sees an answer at once (the behaviour store.ts has today); (2) if a provider is ready, the model gets the findings and the tools, and may call tools for up to the AI-04 limits, with parallel tool calls run concurrently on the one snapshot; (3) text streams through the checker; (4) repair or fallback per G5. Effort maps EFFORTS (low…max) to each provider's reasoning setting; on a 400 that names the setting, retry once without it (what route.ts does). Model and effort are per run; the default comes from settings.

Done when

with the fake model scripted to call neighbors then answer, the SSE shows the calls, the checked sentences and usage; scripted bad citations trigger exactly one repair; a scripted provider failure yields the engine answer with the "model call failed" notice.

Security

no tool writes, runs code or reaches the network (G6). Tool names from the model are matched against the five-name allow list; unknown names return an error result to the model and count against the limit. Model output is never executed, never used as a URL, never used as a file path.

Scale

worst case 6 round trips at ultra is 6 × 100k input tokens; prompt caching (AI-11) makes rounds 2–6 mostly cached, and a per-run input ceiling (default 400k tokens, in settings) stops the run with a notice.

Efficiency

the engine-first step removes one or two round trips for the four common plans. Tool results use the concise format.

Size

L.

Tests

scripted-model end-to-end for each branch (tool use, repair, fallback, stop, limit hit, provider error); golden SSE transcripts.

Full text of AI-14

Depends on (as written)

AI-07, AI-13.

What to build

budgets: base 8,000 tokens; ultra min(context_window / 2, 100_000) (G8), where the window comes from the registry or the provider, default 64,000 when unknown (today's constant). Estimation before sending: characters ÷ a per-model factor, starting at 4 and corrected by a running ratio of reported to estimated tokens, stored per model in ask.db. No tokenizer dependency at first. Usage after: input, output, cached-read and cached-write tokens from the provider, stored per turn and sent in the usage event. Cost is unspecified anywhere; proposed: show tokens always, and show money only when the registry entry has prices (input_per_mtok, output_per_mtok, cached_per_mtok). Prices are never fetched from the network. Local providers show "local".

Done when

the inspector's budget bar shows estimated blocks against the budget and, after the run, the provider's real totals; an Ollama model with an 8k window gets a 4k ultra budget, not 32k.

Security

the per-run token ceiling and output cap bound spend if a loop misbehaves.

Scale

up to 100k-token contexts.

Efficiency

estimation is O(length); no tokenizer tables in the binary.

Size

S.

Tests

unit (budget maths, the Ollama case); property (the packer never exceeds the budget by the estimate).

AI-16

Ultra: the budget packer with personalised PageRank M

Depends on AI-02 AI-14 SRV-11

pack.rs, a port of rank and buildContext from lib/ai.ts.

Done when: on the northwind index the packed set equals the TS packer's set for the golden questions; on the 60k/200k fixture packing takes under 100 ms p95.

Scale

Efficiency

Full text of AI-16

Depends on (as written)

AI-02, AI-14; SRV-11 active links at a date as an adjacency list.

What to build

pack.rs, a port of rank and buildContext from lib/ai.ts. Seeds are the evidence nodes. Rank by personalised PageRank (damping 0.85) over links active at at. Greedily add rows while the estimate fits the budget. Report left_out. Order blocks so the evidence sits at the start and the question at the end (G8 cites "Lost in the Middle"). Each added row gets a handle and a reached_by: "pagerank" tag for the inspector.

Done when

on the northwind index the packed set equals the TS packer's set for the golden questions; on the 60k/200k fixture packing takes under 100 ms p95.

Security

more context means more untrusted text; every added row goes through AI-11's delimiters. The node cap bounds memory.

Scale

200k links: a CSR adjacency is about 2 MB; 20 power iterations are about 4 M edge visits, a few milliseconds. If file-level graphs grow past about 2 M links, switch to forward-push approximate PageRank, which touches only the neighbourhood of the seeds.

Efficiency

the CSR for (fact_hash, day) is cached, 4 entries LRU, so a thread's follow-ups reuse it.

Size

M.

Tests

golden against TS; property (rank sums to 1; budget never exceeded; seeds always included).

Full text of FE-20

Depends on (as written)

FE-03, FE-05, AI-13.

What to build

  • Delete the browser engine: lib/ask.ts's planner and graph walks, and lib/ai.ts's buildContext, SYSTEM, sseDeltas, narrate, checkCitations use. Citations are checked only in Rust (G5). The client renders what the server marks.
  • components/ask/store.ts keeps its shape (one store, three surfaces: docked, floating, /ask) and changes its source:
    • Threads and turns come from GET /api/ask/threads (G9). localStorage keeps only placement: mode, float, returnTo.
    • run() posts to POST /api/ask/runs and reads the event stream: plan, tool call, tool result, text, citation result, repair, done, error. The run lives on the server and has an id, so any surface or tab can attach with GET /api/ask/runs/:id/events?after=<seq>. That keeps the "ask here, watch there" behaviour and survives a reload.
    • stop() aborts the fetch and calls DELETE /api/ask/runs/:id.
    • Old local threads were answers about demo data. They're dropped on first run of the new build, with one line in the changelog.
  • Message.tsx renders checked, dropped and unsupported sentences from the server's verdicts, the "set aside" notice when the repair fails, and the "Written by a model" chip.   • Model picker and Models settings: providers and models from the API; "Save key" posts once and the field clears; the UI learns only key_set: true (G4). One-time migration: if cartograph:ai holds keys, post them to the keychain route, then delete the entry.
  • History (D15): past questions, re-run as of another date, stale marker when cited ids are gone, "Forget all".
  • With no model set, Ask still answers from the graph engine through the same route.

answers render as plain text; links come only from checked citations; no markdown images or model-written URLs are ever rendered (G6). Tool results shown in the inspector are untrusted repo text inside <pre>. The key field uses autocomplete="off" and is never echoed. The base URL for a custom provider is validated and stored by the server, so the browser can't steer requests per call.

Scale

turns up to the G8 budget (about 100k tokens of context shown in the inspector, virtualised); threads paged at 30.

Efficiency

stream chunks are batched per animation frame; the inspector loads on open, not with the turn.

Size

AI-19

Full text of AI-19

Depends on (as written)

XS-10 the shared scanner and its hook before text is stored; AI-11.

What to build

a SecretScanner built from the gitleaks default rule set (check its licence before vendoring the rules) plus user patterns in cartograph.yaml, compiled to one RegexSet. It runs in two places: at index time, before docstrings and other text enter FTS or summaries (owned with XS-10); and as a last gate on every outbound prompt in prompt.rs, replacing matches with [secret removed]. Hits are stored as file, rule and a masked preview for the S06 list (SECRET_HITS today), never the value.

Done when

a fixture repo with planted AWS, Stripe and RSA key material produces the three S06 rows; a canary secret in a docstring never appears in any prompt captured by the fake model, nor in ask.db.

Security

this is the control for "left out of every model prompt" on S06. Regex scanning misses unknown formats; the UI copy should not promise more than "anything that looks like a key".

Scale

scanning text for 60k files and their history: the RegexSet is linear in input; cache results by blob id (B6) so each blob is scanned once.

Efficiency

prefilter by keyword (as gitleaks does) before running the full set.

Size

M.

Tests

golden against gitleaks' own test vectors; the canary end-to-end; a performance gate.

Full text of AI-20

Depends on (as written)

AI-07, AI-09, AI-10.

What to build

S06 shows "Only this machine: Active" as a status. Proposed meaning, two facts computed by the server and shown as they are: (a) the index and MCP are loopback-only (always true, from SRV-05); (b) whether Ask can send text off the machine, which is true when the selected provider is not local. Add one setting, ai.local_only (default off): when on, the policy refuses any non-loopback provider for Ask and summaries, and the picker greys them out. With no provider, the page says what G15 says: nothing leaves this machine. Before the first run against a remote provider, a one-time notice names the host that will receive code-derived text.

Done when

with local_only on, a run against anthropic fails before any connection is opened (asserted with a socket counter) and Ollama works; GET /api/ai/privacy reports both facts.

Summaries L

Depends on AI-07 AI-11 AI-19 IDX-21 SRV-13

off by default (G7). A job, started from Settings or cartograph summarize (command name unspecified; proposed), that writes a plain description per service and module, or also per file (board G15's two choices).

Done when: enabling summaries on the fixture writes one row per service and module; a second run with nothing changed makes zero model calls; changing one file re-summarises that file and its ancestors only; every summary shows the "Written…

Full text of AI-21

Depends on (as written)

AI-07, AI-11, AI-19; IDX-21 derived rows with inputs_key (B6); SRV-13 background jobs and the activity list (I5).

What to build

off by default (G7). A job, started from Settings or cartograph summarize (command name unspecified; proposed), that writes a plain description per service and module, or also per file (board G15's two choices). Bottom-up: a file summary from structure and docstrings; a module from its files' summaries and links; a service from its modules. Cache key: for a file, blob id + model id + prompt version (G7); for a module or service, a hash of its children's keys and its link digest + model id + prompt version (B6 inputs_key). A changed key marks the row stale; the old text stays until rewritten. Proposed storage: cache/summaries.db keyed by inputs_key, materialised into the index, so index --rebuild does not throw away 25 minutes of model time (R7). Each row keeps model, input count and date for the G13 provenance line ("llama3.1 8B · from 14 files · 6 Nov 2026"). The job shows progress, can be cancelled and resumes. The page suggests a local model and shows the count and a time estimate first.

Done when

enabling summaries on the fixture writes one row per service and module; a second run with nothing changed makes zero model calls; changing one file re-summarises that file and its ancestors only; every summary shows the "Written by a model" chip.

Security

this sends the most repo text to a model, so local_only and the secret scan apply, and a remote provider needs an explicit confirm naming the host and the token estimate. Output is untrusted (its input was repo text): stored as text, datamarked when fed to Ask, never placed in tool descriptions, MCP instructions or CARTOGRAPH.md (F8).

Scale

board G15: 1,202 summaries in about 25 minutes on a local model. At 60k files, file-level summaries are about 60k calls; default to services and modules, and require a confirm with the estimate for files.

Efficiency

concurrency 1 for local models, 4 for remote; batch small files into one call; skip generated, binary and over-1 MB files (spec §8).

Size

L.

Tests

cache-key unit tests; the incremental golden; cancel and resume; the canary secret.

Phase 7 · Harden and ship

19 items · 5S 9M 5L · roadmap stage 7, and the release
Goal

Finish the fuzz, fault and large-repository work that started with each parser, close the last app items, and produce signed, attested binaries with a docs site whose commands are run in CI.

What a user can do at the end

Install with the shell installer, Homebrew or npm on a clean machine, run cartograph doctor clean, and follow the get-started page from install to a map. Copy a share link and open the same view on another machine.

Exit gate
  • Every fuzz target has 24 CPU-hours with no open crash; the kill loop passes 1,000 iterations on three operating systems.
  • Every row of the large-repository matrix finishes inside its budget three nights running.
  • axe reports no violation on any route in both themes and in More contrast; the screen-reader script passes on VoiceOver and NVDA.
  • With the network blocked, only sync --fetch and Ask open a socket to a non-loopback address.
  • A tag produces signed, attested artefacts for every target; verifying a tampered archive fails.
Risks retired
  • Untrusted repo content reaching C parsers (tree-sitter, SQLite).
  • A tampered release.
  • Format changes stranding old indexes.

Fuzz targets (HD-07), failpoints (HD-08) and per-area gates start with the code they cover; this phase is where they must all be green. SRV-20 and AI-23 are opt-in surfaces and don't block the release. Publishing is blocked on the product name.

HD-07

Fuzzing (ROADMAP stage 7) L

Depends on HD-02

cargo fuzz targets, each asserting no panic, no hang past 1 s, and bounded memory (256 MB)

Security
this is the main defence for "untrusted repo content". Run with AddressSanitizer for targets that touch C (tree-sitter, SQLite).

Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-07

Depends on (as written)

the parsers existing.

What to build

cargo fuzz targets, each asserting no panic, no hang past 1 s, and bounded memory (256 MB):

TargetInputWhy
jsonc_workspace.code-workspace bytesC1; first file the tool reads
manifest_yaml, overrides_yamlconfig bytesB3, H3; YAML bombs (anchors, deep nesting)
spec_openapi, spec_asyncapi, spec_proto, spec_graphqlspec bytes$ref cycles, huge enums
extract_<lang>source bytes per tree-sitter grammaruntrusted repo content; grammars are C code
scm_queryadapter .scm filesH4
ndjson_factadapter subprocess outputH4; an adapter is third-party code
git_objectsa repo directory through gixa cloned repo is attacker-controlled
http_requestraw request bytes, Host, Origin, pathA3; path traversal in the static handler
view_linkcartograph:// stringsI7; input from another person
mcp_frameJSON-RPC frames on stdioF1
provider_streammodel SSE bytesG3; a provider or proxy can send anything
citation_textmodel text with handlesG5
sqlite_opena damaged index fileB3; must reach the G02 error, not a crash

Differential fuzzing where a reference exists: JSONC against jsonc-parser's own tests and VS Code's behaviour; replay against direct indexing. Corpora are seeded from the fixtures and kept in the repo. Pull requests run each target for 60 s; nightly for 30 minutes each. The front-end parsers (viewState, SSE) are fuzzed with fast-check and Jazzer.js.

Done when

every target has run 24 CPU-hours with no open crash; crashes found become regression tests.

Security

this is the main defence for "untrusted repo content". Run with AddressSanitizer for targets that touch C (tree-sitter, SQLite).

Scale

inputs capped at 2 MB, the board S03 file-size limit.

HD-08

Crash and fault injection (ROADMAP stage 7) L

Depends on IDX-13 SRV-05

a harness, xtask chaos, using the fail crate's failpoints and process signals

Done when: the harness passes on Linux, macOS and Windows in the nightly job; every failure mode above has a named test.

Merge note: Owns the failpoint harness that IDX-13, IDX-16 and SRV-13 place failpoints for.

Security
crash reports hold paths, versions and stack frames, never file contents, keys or environment values.
Scale
run on acme-scale, so interrupts land in every phase.
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-08

Depends on (as written)

IDX-07, SRV-05.

What to build

a harness, xtask chaos, using the fail crate's failpoints and process signals:

  • SIGKILL at a random point in a sync, 1,000 times. After each: the index opens, shows the last committed batch, the lock is free (B2's advisory lock), and the next sync finishes with the same fact hash as a clean run.
  • SIGINT and Cancel: the previous index stays readable and parsed blobs stay cached (E5).
  • Disk full, read-only file system, and failed fsync at each write site.
  • Truncated and bit-flipped index files: the result is G02's "can't be read" with the rebuild command, never a panic.
  • Two syncs at once: the second sets the dirty flag and exits 0; the first runs one more round (D2).
  • A reader holding a snapshot during a write and during a WAL truncate (B2).
  • Full rebuild interrupted during the rename (B2).
  • Registry write interrupted (C4).
  • Ask: provider timeout, 429, malformed stream, a stream cut mid-token, and the user's Stop. Each ends in a stored turn and a clear message.
  • Front end: binary killed while the app is open, SSE cut, a response for an old generation arriving late.
  • Panic handling: a panic in one extractor on one file marks that file unparsed and the run continues. A local crash report is written with code left out (X13); nothing is sent.

Done when

the harness passes on Linux, macOS and Windows in the nightly job; every failure mode above has a named test.

Security

crash reports hold paths, versions and stack frames, never file contents, keys or environment values. They stay on disk until the user attaches one.

Scale

run on acme-scale, so interrupts land in every phase.

Size

L

Tests

itself.

What to build

  • Pinned public repos at three sizes (research "Next"), run nightly on a quiet runner with at least ten runs; CI fails when a gate regresses by more than 15%.
  • Gates (targets marked proposed are replaced by IDX-02 measurements):
GateTargetSource
Freshness check, 50 reposunder 100 ms p95D1
Incremental sync, 100 commits (remotion)under 500 ms warmResearch: 105 ms in memory; proposed with disk
Full index, remotion, cold cacheunder 60 sProposed; research: 4.6 s with a regular expression
Full index, remotion, warm cacheunder 10 sProposed
Replay resolutions, remotionabout 400k, within 10%Measured; restated from the research's 60,039, which keyed on file dependencies only
As-of link query, 200k links, from diskunder 5 msProposed; research: 0.06 ms in memory over 36,193
Peak memory, 60k files and 200k linksunder 1.5 GBProposed
Rename detection overhead on the walkunder 2× the walk aloneProposed; B7 benchmark
Fact hash, 412 MB indexunder 2 sProposed
  • Fuzz targets: JSONC, YAML manifest, registry TOML, each extractor, the index opener, the tree-diff reader.
  • Fault injection: kill points in the writer, disk-full simulation, a read-only data folder, a lock held by a suspended process.

RepositoryWhat it stresses

SRV-25

cartograph report S

Depends on SRV-01 IDX-13

A panic hook and an error recorder that write a structured record to logs/.

Done when: a forced extractor failure gives a report that contains none of the fixture's file names, code or remote URL (asserted by search).

Scale
logs/ rotates at 10 MB; 20 reports are kept.
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of SRV-25

Depends on (as written)

SRV-01, IDX (failure records).

What to build

  • A panic hook and an error recorder that write a structured record to logs/.
  • cartograph report builds reports/<timestamp>.md from the last failure: version, OS, the failing step, the extractor and its version, the error chain, and counts.
  • Redaction: paths become <repo-1>/<depth-3>.ts style placeholders, no source text, no commit messages, no remote URLs, no keys.
  • It prints the path and the issue URL. It never opens a network connection.

Done when

a forced extractor failure gives a report that contains none of the fixture's file names, code or remote URL (asserted by search).

Security

the report is the one artefact a user is asked to share, so redaction is on an allowlist: only known-safe fields are written.

Scale

logs/ rotates at 10 MB; 20 reports are kept.

Efficiency

n/a.

Size

S.

Tests

a redaction test with planted secrets, golden.

What to build

  • cartograph mcp --http [--port N]: Streamable HTTP at /mcp, loopback only, with the same Host and Origin middleware as SRV-05.
  • mcp.token in the data directory: 32 random bytes, hex, mode 0600, created on first use. cartograph mcp token prints it for Claude Code's headersHelper; --rotate replaces it.

HD-13

Signed releases M

Depends on HD-12 SRV-27

cargo-dist (E4) for macOS arm64 and x64, Linux x64 and arm64 (glibc and musl), Windows x64: shell and PowerShell installers, a Homebrew tap, an MSI. On top of it

Done when: a tag produces signed, attested artefacts for five targets; a fresh machine installs through each documented path and runs cartograph doctor clean; verifying a tampered archive fails.

Merge note: Owns signing, notarisation, checksums and attestations (merges SRV-29). Certificates are a call for you.

Security
signing keys never exist as long-lived secrets (keyless); the release workflow can't be triggered from a fork.
Scale
Nothing specific
Efficiency
Nothing specific
Full text of HD-13

Depends on (as written)

HD-12, E4's naming decision.

What to build

cargo-dist (E4) for macOS arm64 and x64, Linux x64 and arm64 (glibc and musl), Windows x64: shell and PowerShell installers, a Homebrew tap, an MSI. On top of it:

  • SHA-256 checksums and GitHub artifact attestations (build provenance) for every artefact; gh attestation verify documented in the install guide.
  • Keyless cosign signatures on the archives and the SBOM.
  • macOS: Developer ID signing and notarisation, or the binary is quarantined when downloaded by a browser. This needs a paid Apple account: undecided. Without it, document Homebrew and cargo install as the supported paths on macOS.
  • Windows: Authenticode signing for the MSI. Needs a certificate: undecided.
  • npm: scoped package with one package per platform (E4), published with provenance.
  • Release job rights: id-token: write and contents: write only in the tagged release workflow, gated by a protected environment.
  • The release build embeds out/ from the same commit, and a test asserts cartograph --version equals the app's version string (A4).
  • No update check. The binary never contacts the network to look for a new version (hard rule). doctor prints the version and the releases URL.

Done when

a tag produces signed, attested artefacts for five targets; a fresh machine installs through each documented path and runs cartograph doctor clean; verifying a tampered archive fails.

Security

signing keys never exist as long-lived secrets (keyless); the release workflow can't be triggered from a fork.

Size

M

Tests

install smoke tests per platform in CI; a verification test.

AI-23

External agent over ACP (later, optional) L

Depends on AI-06 AI-12 SRV-15

a provider kind acp in the registry with a command the user installed (claude-agent-acp, Codex, Gemini CLI).

Done when: with a scripted ACP agent, an answer streams into Ask with checked citations; a scripted request to run a shell command is rejected and logged; no credential appears in any Cartograph file.

Merge note: After stage 6 ships, optional, and shipped per agent once a spike proves that agent's shell and file tools are off.

Security
see R8. An ACP agent has its own built-in tools that do not go through the client, and its own settings may allow them without asking.
Scale
one subprocess per run; kill on Stop and on timeout.
Efficiency
the agent process is reused within a thread.
Full text of AI-23

Depends on (as written)

AI-06, AI-12; SRV-15 a read-only mode; stage 6 shipped (G2). Re-read Anthropic's policy first (G1 says it has changed several times).

What to build

a provider kind acp in the registry with a command the user installed (claude-agent-acp, Codex, Gemini CLI). Cartograph spawns it, speaks ACP over stdio, declares no file-system and no terminal capability, and passes one MCP server to session/new: itself, as cartograph mcp --read-only --ask-run <id>, which exposes the six read tools and writes handles for that run to ask.db so AI-12 can check the answer. Every permission request that is not for a Cartograph tool is rejected. Turns are capped. The agent's working directory is an empty temp folder, not a repo. Sign-in happens inside the agent; Cartograph shows no sign-in button and stores nothing.

Done when

with a scripted ACP agent, an answer streams into Ask with checked citations; a scripted request to run a shell command is rejected and logged; no credential appears in any Cartograph file.

Security

see R8. An ACP agent has its own built-in tools that do not go through the client, and its own settings may allow them without asking. Rejecting permission requests is necessary but may not be sufficient; the empty working directory and agent-specific flags to disable tools are needed, and the UI must say that the external agent runs under its own rules. Never route plan credentials; never call claude -p (G2).

Scale

one subprocess per run; kill on Stop and on timeout.

Efficiency

the agent process is reused within a thread.

Size

L.

Tests

a scripted ACP peer; permission-rejection tests; a process-leak test.

Critical path

what holds everything else up

Longest dependency chain, weighted by size: about 28 item-weeks

HD-01 → HD-02 → IDX-01 → IDX-02 → IDX-05 → IDX-06 → IDX-07 → SRV-02 → SRV-04 → SRV-05 → SRV-07 → SRV-08 → SRV-11 → SRV-15 → AI-02 → AI-03 → AI-04 → AI-13 → AI-14 → AI-16 → AI-17 → FE-20

Computed from the dependencies on this page. It runs through the query layer and the graph slice into MCP and then Ask, not through the cross-service work, because Ask's tools are the MCP tools.

1

Benchmarks, then the replay engine

IDX-02 → IDX-04, IDX-05 → IDX-06 → IDX-07. Nothing can read an index until one exists. IDX-06 is the item most exposed to a failed spike.

2

The contract, the query layer and the server

INT-01 → SRV-02 → SRV-04 → SRV-05 → SRV-07 → FE-02 → FE-03 → FE-06. The route table had no owner before this merge; agreeing it is the first thing to do.

3

Workspace breadth and layout

IDX-08 → IDX-11 → IDX-12 → IDX-14 → IDX-20 → IDX-25 → FE-08, FE-09. Compare and scrubbing only work with stored, stable positions. If layout slips, they slip.

4

Read API, then MCP

SRV-08 → SRV-11 → SRV-15. The tool registry built here is the one Ask registers from.

5

Cross-service links

XS-01 → XS-03 → XS-04 → XS-05 → XS-08 → XS-09 → XS-16 → XS-20 → XS-21. XS-09 is the largest item here and can't be cut: without it most real links stay guessed.

6

Ask, then the release

AI-02 → AI-04 → AI-13 → FE-20 → SRV-27 → HD-13 → HD-15. Publishing waits on the product name.

Beside

Work that runs beside the path without blocking it

FE-07 (sigma bench), FE-12 (table view, needs only FE-06), FE-13 (settings, needs only the config routes), SRV-21 and SRV-22 (agent setup), IDX-26 and IDX-27 (hooks and the hourly fetch), AI-07 to AI-09 (providers, keys, outbound policy: they need only AI-01), HD-12, and HD-15's generated reference pages.

Security model

one threat table, merged across the five areas

Attackers considered: a web page open in the same browser, another user on the machine, a hostile repository in the workspace, a teammate's committed file, a third-party adapter, a model provider or proxy, and a tampered release. A process running as the same user is out of scope: it can already read the index, and SECURITY.md says so. The index is not encrypted; file permissions are the control.

ThreatWhereMitigationOwner
A web page calls the local API (CSRF)open, mcp --httpBearer header required, which a form or image can't send. No CORS, so a preflight fails. Origin and Sec-Fetch-Site checks. Writes need a JSON content type.SRV-05 SRV-20 HD-11
DNS rebindingsameHost allow list: 127.0.0.1, localhost, [::1] with the exact port.SRV-05
Exposure on the LANsameLoopback bind only. Any other host is refused with a reason. A test connects on the LAN address.SRV-05 HD-11
Another local user reads data or connectsport, data folder256-bit token compared in constant time. Data folder 0700, files 0600, umask 077, an ACL on Windows.SRV-05 SRV-26 IDX-03
Token leaksbrowserCarried in the fragment, never the query string. Removed from the URL at boot. Held in sessionStorage, never localStorage, logs, error text or share links. Referrer-Policy: no-referrer.SRV-05 FE-03
Cookies shared across local portsbrowserNo cookies at all.SRV-05
Script injection from repo textappNames, paths, snippets, commit text and model text render as text nodes. Strict CSP with hashed inline scripts. /source is text/plain with nosniff. No autolinking.SRV-06 FE-01 FE-06 FE-20
Data leaving through the browserappconnect-src 'self'. Model calls are made by Rust. The end-to-end suite runs with outbound network blocked.SRV-06 HD-11 HD-05
Path traversalstatic files, /source, fs/list, write routes, working tree, specs, SCIPEmbedded key lookup for assets. Blobs by id from the index, never a path from the request. Canonicalise and refuse symlinks. Writes only at three fixed file names. $ref and tsconfig extends resolve inside the git tree, never the disk.SRV-06 SRV-08 SRV-14 IDX-06 IDX-24 XS-08 XS-27
Folder listing discloses the diskwizardNames and three flags only, never contents. No symlinks, no dot-folders, home and named volumes only, token required.SRV-14 FE-15
SQL or FTS injectionquery layerStatic SQL with bound parameters. Search tokens quoted by us. Cursors validated and never trusted as SQL.SRV-02 SRV-09 AI-15
A crafted index fileopenapplication_id check, trusted_schema = OFF, defensive mode, no extensions, no triggers or views, quick_check, a fuzz target.IDX-03 HD-07
A hostile repositoryindexerNever runs repo-configured programs (no hooks, filters, credential helpers, build tools). Reads the object store, not the checkout. Blob size cap, parse time limit, rename candidate limit, row caps. Grammars vendored and pinned.IDX-04 IDX-05 IDX-18 IDX-28
A teammate's manifest redirects writes or bombs the parsercartograph.yaml, override fileNo field is executed or expanded. index_path honoured only from user config. Size cap and alias-expansion limit. Regexes compile with a size limit and no backtracking.IDX-10 XS-12 XS-22
A manifest names a command to runsubprocess adaptersRuns only after adapters allow, with the hash kept outside any repo. Sandbox with no network and no file access; refused where no sandbox exists. Content on stdin, scrubbed environment, time and output limits, every fact validated.XS-24
Prompt injection through MCPtools, CARTOGRAPH.md, hook lineDescriptions, instructions and the skill are compile-time constants. Repo text only in untrusted_text, on request, truncated. Names restricted to a safe character set. status --line has no names.SRV-15 SRV-23 SRV-03 SRV-22
Prompt injection through AskAsk loopTools are five read-only graph tools. Repo text sits inside per-thread random delimiters and is datamarked. Answers are plain text; links come only from checked citations. The checker is deterministic, so injected text can't talk it round.AI-02 AI-11 AI-12 AI-22
Server-side request forgery through a model base URLAskThe base URL is a saved setting, never a request field. https, or http to loopback or a listed host. No redirects. Resolve, validate, pin. Link-local and our own port always refused. A key is bound to the origin it was saved for.AI-09
A model key leaksresponses, logs, reports, ask.db, shell historyKeychain storage. Entered on stdin or once through the API, never returned. A redactor on every error body, event, log line and report. A canary key scanned for across all outputs.AI-08 AI-10
Secrets in code reach the index or a promptconfig values, snippets, search, summariesConfig values stored only if they classify as a locator; URLs lose userinfo and query. One scanner masks evidence text, raw references and search text, and gates every outbound prompt.XS-10 AI-19
Runaway spend or loopAskPer run: 6 model round trips, 12 tool calls, 120 s, 2,000 output tokens, a 400k input ceiling. Two runs per process.AI-04 AI-13 AI-14
Resource exhaustionAPI, MCP, traversalsRow, byte and time limits on every query. Body limit 1 MB, 64 concurrent requests, 16 streams. Traversal caps of 50k nodes and depth 12. Response cap with a named narrower scope.SRV-02 SRV-04 SRV-05 SRV-11
A long reader blocks WAL truncationquery layerNo snapshot outlives a request; 30 s hard cap; the checkpoint falls back to passive and the WAL is size-limited.SRV-02 IDX-13

Scale targets and performance gates

measured · decided · proposed

Measured comes from docs/research/README.md on remotion (35,439 commits, 9,498 first-parent) with a regular expression in place of a parser and no disk writes. Decided is a number in the decision record. Proposed is an analyst's default, to be replaced by a phase 0 measurement. Where two analysts proposed different numbers for one thing, one is kept here and the other is listed under Rulings.
