Gate method (HD-09): shared CI runners are noisy, so counts and instruction-level benches gate pull requests at ±3%, and wall-clock gates compare base and head interleaved in one job, failing above 10%. Absolute numbers are checked nightly on one fixed machine and published under docs/research/. The timings in the spec's examples (17,207 commits in 40 minutes, 31,900 in 3 minutes, a rebuild in 8.7 s) are made up and are not targets.

Interfaces

the contracts between areas, after the rulings

HTTP API

Base http://127.0.0.1:<port>, same origin as the app. Every /api/* request sends Authorization: Bearer <token>. JSON, snake_case. Workspace routes sit under /api/v1/w/{ws}, shown below as …. Reads take at (a date, a commit sha or now), limit (default 50, maximum 1,000), cursor and include. Envelope: {"schema":1,"generation":418,"data":{},"next_cursor":null,"truncated":false}. Error: {"schema":1,"error":{"code","what","why","try","details"}}. Response headers: Cartograph-Generation, Cartograph-State, ETag.

RouteKindReturns or doesOwner
GET /api/version · GET /api/v1/metareadVersion, format version, now, schema (A4)SRV-05
GET /api/v1/workspaces · /workspaces/{ws}readWorkspaceSummary from the registrySRV-05
GET /api/v1/eventsstreamOne stream for every workspace (events below)SRV-13
GET …/freshness · …/reposreadFreshness, Repo[]SRV-03
GET …/graph?lod=&scope=&at= or &range=readGraphSlice, columnar; min_confidence, kinds, owner, repo, branch filtersSRV-04
GET …/nodes/{id} · /children · /metrics · /series · /neighbors · /changes · /ranking · /history · /symbols · /reading-orderreadPanel and inspector data; lineage for board G10SRV-08
GET …/links/{id}readLinkDetail: evidence, level, score, winning layer, interval, replaced-bySRV-08 XS-06
GET …/symbols/{id}readCallers and callees grouped by fileXS-25
GET …/source?node=&commit=&lines=readBlob text by id, text/plain, 400 lines and 64 KB at mostSRV-08
GET …/search?q=&kinds=&at=&limit=readSearchResult[] grouped by kind; also the Ask @ pickerSRV-09
GET …/commits · /commits/{sha} · /commits/{sha}/impactreadCommits paged; CommitImpact with prev and nextSRV-10 XS-21
GET …/timeline/density · /timeline/markersreadBins by day, week or month; renames and rewritesSRV-10
GET …/impact?node= · POST …/impactreadAffected nodes as exact, likely, guessed. The POST only carries a body (paths, ref, working tree, since); it changes nothingSRV-11

The write list is closed. Every route marked write above needs the token, a matching Origin and Content-Type: application/json. Files are written only at cartograph.yaml, cartograph.overrides.yaml and cartograph.baseline.json in the workspace folder, plus user config. No route writes the index: jobs ask the indexer to. There is no base_url and no api_key field in any request except the provider and key writes.

Event streams

Both are text/event-stream read with fetch, since EventSource can't send the bearer header. Each event has an id: sequence number. Heartbeat comment every 15 s.

StreamEvents
GET /api/v1/eventsfreshness {ws, freshness} · job.started {job_id, ws, kind, by} · job.progress {job_id, stage, done, total, repo?, eta_s?} (at most 4 a second) · job.finished {job_id, ok, generation?, took_ms, error?} · index.changed {ws, generation, fact_hash} · config.changed {ws, etag, file} · activity.new · workspaces.changed. The current state is sent on connect.
POST …/ask/runsrun.started · plan · tool.call {call_id, tool, args, by} · tool.result {call_id, rows, handles, ms} · answer (the engine's, before any model text) · budget · tail · sentence {p, text, cites, supported} · answer.replace · usage · notice · error · done {status, stats}

MCP tools

stdio by default. Every inputSchema is a flat object with additionalProperties: false and no top-level oneOf. Every result carries schema, freshness, data, next_cursor, truncated; content is compact Markdown ending in one freshness line. Resource: cartograph://status. Ask registers the five marked tools from the same registry.

Index schema, format version 1

All tables are STRICT. Everything that changes over time is an interval [from_seq, to_seq) on the merged first-parent timeline. A link is live at step s when from_seq <= s AND (to_seq IS NULL OR to_seq > s); a date maps to the step with the greatest corrected time at or below it. The full DDL is in IDX-03's source plan.

Conflict rulings

30 decided · 8 need your call

Needs your call

Each of these changes what the product does or promises, and the decision record doesn't settle it. The plan proceeds on the recommended default until you say otherwise.

N1

The product name Needs your call Blocks release

E4 and PRODUCT.md both call "Cartograph" a placeholder. The crate, npm scope, Homebrew tap, plugin name, cartograph:// scheme, file extension, application id, config paths and hook names all embed it.

Recommended default: keep the name in one constants module from IDX-01 on, and decide before SRV-27. Nothing before phase 7 is blocked. Why it's yours: it's a naming and trademark choice.

N2

A local_only setting behind "Only this machine" Needs your call

Board S06 shows a static "Active" chip that nothing enforces. AI-20 proposes one setting that makes the outbound policy refuse any non-loopback provider for Ask and summaries, plus a one-time notice naming the host before the first remote run.

Recommended default: add it, off by default, enforced in the outbound policy and not in the UI. Why it's yours: it's a new user-facing promise and changes the board.

N3

Direction of an event link between services Needs your call

H3's retarget example reads as publisher to consumer. Every other link kind, and the demo data, read as "from depends on to", which makes it consumer to publisher.

Recommended default: store publishes and subscribes edges to the channel, which are unambiguous. Draw the service-level link consumer to publisher, like every other kind. In the override file, read an event key's from and to by name as publisher and consumer, so H3's example stays valid. Why it's yours: it decides how H3's example is read and what the arrow means on the map.

N4

Code2DFD is believed to be Java Needs your call

H2 measures precision per level on the 17 Code2DFD applications. The cross-service analyst believes they are Java and Spring, which has no extractor on the roadmap, and did not verify it. INT-02 checks in phase 0.

Recommended default: gate CI on a hand-labelled TypeScript, Go and Python set from the start; write Java .scm adapters inside XS-07 and publish Code2DFD figures when they exist; don't show "Exact" for an evidence type until it has been measured on one of the two sets. Why it's yours: the alternative is to hold the claim until Code2DFD runs, which costs up to an L item.

N5

Commits that arrive dated before the watermark Needs your call

The merged timeline orders repos by corrected commit date. A late fetch or a repo added later brings commits dated in the past. No decision covers it.

Recommended default: partial replay from that date (IDX-14), so "the map as of a date" stays true across repos. The alternative appends at the watermark and accepts a mismatch between date and order. Why it's yours: it defines what a date on the timeline means.

N6

Signing certificates Needs your call

macOS notarisation needs a paid Apple Developer ID; a signed MSI needs an Authenticode certificate.

Recommended default: ship keyless signatures, checksums and attestations for every platform; on macOS document Homebrew and cargo install as the supported paths until a Developer ID exists; decide before the first public binary (HD-13). Why it's yours: it's a purchase and an account.

N7

Concept decisions in the override file Needs your call

Board A4's "Keep separate" and "Same concept" need somewhere to persist. XS-28 proposes a concepts: section in cartograph.overrides.yaml, which extends H3's schema beyond link corrections.

Recommended default: allow the section, each entry with a reason, under the same write path as corrections. Why it's yours: it amends H3.

N8

Does a dropped connection cancel an Ask run Needs your call

AI-04 cancels a run when the client disconnects, so a reload ends it. FE-20 wants the run to live on the server so any surface or tab can reattach with GET …/ask/runs/{id}/events?after=.

Recommended default: only Stop cancels. A run outlives its connection, bounded by the 120 s and token limits, and can be reattached. Why it's yours: it trades a little possible spend after a closed tab for an answer that survives a reload.

Decided

Each ruling is consistent with the decision record or the hard rules. Two of them ask for a wording change in the record (A1), marked below.
